Now turning to the drivers. Consolidated revenue for the third quarter of 2026 was $54.5 million compared with $104.3 million in the third quarter of 2025. Third quarter consolidated revenue consisted of $17.2 million in DefenCath net sales and $37.3 million from the remainder of the portfolio, consisting of $31.4 million of product sales, and approximately $5.9 million of contract revenue and grant income under our BARDA agreement.

The year over year decline in DefenCath revenue primarily reflects the lower net pricing associated with its transition on July 1, to the post-TDAPA reimbursement period. Importantly, large outpatient dialysis customers largely maintained utilization during the quarter despite the reimbursement change. DefenCath net revenue included a $5 million favorable change in estimate related to certain Medicaid rebate and product return allowances. The adjustment reflects updated information regarding Medicaid utilization as well as recent DefendCast product return experience. Both of which have been much lower than previously estimated. Total operating expenses were $36.6 million in the third quarter of 2026, compared with $41.7 million in the third quarter of 2025. A decrease of approximately 12%.

The reduction primarily reflects merger related and reorganization costs incurred following the Melinta acquisition in the prior year period, which was partially offset by the inclusion of a full quarter of costs associated with the expanded organization in 2026 versus just 1 month of Melinta's costs in the third quarter of 2025. R&D expenses were $7 million in the quarter, compared with $5.1 million in the third quarter of 2025. The increase was primarily attributable to higher personnel and clinical trial costs supporting our combined company ongoing development programs. Selling and marketing expenses were $11.7 million in the quarter, compared with $11.2 million in the prior year quarter.

The modest increase reflects personnel marketing investments supporting the expanded product portfolio partially offset by the cost savings associated with the elimination of overlapping commercial teams that were present for a portion of the third quarter of 2025. G&A expenses decreased to $17.9 million from $25.5 million in the third quarter of 2025. The decrease year over year was largely attributable to transaction integration, and reorganization expenses incurred in connection with the Melinta acquisition during the prior year period and partially offset by higher costs in 2026 of operating the combined company. Including investments in personnel, IT, legal support, and facilities.

On the bottom line, on a GAAP basis, CorMedix reported a net loss of $14.7 million or $0.19 per basic and diluted share compared with net income of $108.6 million or $1.42 per basic share and $1.26 per diluted share in the third quarter of 2025. A large driver of net income in the prior year period was a substantial tax benefit of $59.7 million associated with the partial reversal of the valuation allowance on deferred tax assets. Third quarter 2026 GAAP results included an $11.5 million noncash expense from the quarterly remeasurement of contingent consideration associated with the Melinta acquisition.

This expense reflected changes in the discount rates applied to expected milestone and royalty payments as well as accretion of the liability from the passage of time. On a non GAAP basis, adjusted EBITDA was $3.1 million for the third quarter of 2026 compared with $71.9 million in the third quarter of 2025. Adjusted EBITDA excludes depreciation and amortization, stock based compensation, interest and other income and expense, changes in contingent consideration, taxes, merger related costs, and other specified adjustments. The third quarter of 2026, the calculation also excludes the $5 million favorable change in estimate related to sales allowances reported during the quarter. We ended the quarter with $355.2 million in cash and cash equivalents.

For the first 9 months of 2026, net cash provided by operating activities was $218.3 million compared with $80.6 million in the first 9 months of 2025. The year over year increase in cash flow reflects the higher year to date earnings achieved during the current year relative to 2025, as well as working capital movements, including the timing of certain contractual rebate payments. As discussed earlier, we are maintaining our full-year 2026 consolidated revenue guidance of $325 million to $345 million and we are narrowing our adjusted EBITDA guidance to $130 million to $140 million the high end of our previous range.

We have also lowered and narrowed our full year operating expense guidance which excludes noncash items such as stock based compensation, up to $140 million to $145 million. Warhammer 40,000: Darktide has announced its newest class, the Sister Novitiate, is arriving on December 7, finally bringing the much anticipated Adepta Sororitas faction to Atoma. Alongside the Skitarii, which was the last class added, the Sisters of Battle have been long–requested by players.

Also similar to the Skitarii, this class has a slightly different design to the other rejects. The Sisters Novitiate can choose from six Acts of Faith instead of blitz and combat abilities, drawing on a new "Faith" resource to use an offensive Neural Whip, or more support based abilities like the Icon of Purity and protective prayers.

Essentially, sisters build their faith through combat via Doctrines and Pledges within the talent tree, which change how it's accrued, allowing them to then unleash up to two chosen Acts of Faith. It sounds fairly similar to the Skitarii's somewhat looser skill and ability tree.

In terms of weapons, we can look forward to:

There's also a meltagun (the first in Darktide), which will be added as a free update alongside for the Veteran class to also use. I'm personally pretty excited for the Condemnor Stake Thrower, a crossbow which pins enemies in place, and as a Zealot main, a new chainsword in the form of the Penitent Eviscerator. There'll likely be a fair bit of crossover between the Zealot and Sister Novitiate.

Overall, this new class is a clever way of including the Adepta Sororitas in Darktide. Lore wise, it would make little sense for a full Sister of Battle to be running around with convicts and rejects, but a sister-in-training, sure, why not?

You can find the new class on Steam where it's set to cost €12 (so I assume £10/$12, the same as the Skitarii was) when it launches on December 7.

Ceramic films are widely used in solid-state batteries, energy conversion devices and electronic systems. Their fabrication, however, often requires high-temperature sintering, which can cause elemental volatilization, compositional segregation and undesirable interfacial reactions. Achieving dense, compositionally uniform ceramic films at lower temperatures therefore remains a challenge.

Missouri State women's basketball eyes big test versus Marquette

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

SPRINGFIELD — Missouri State (3-0) intends on using last year's experience this season.

That is why the Lady Bears' upcoming game is a major early test of the season.

Missouri State hosts Marquette (2-1) at 6:30 p.m. on Friday, Nov. 13.

Last season, the Lady Bears entered the NCAA Tournament as the No. 16 seed. Looking back, the team knows that a few key non-conference wins could've helped their postseason seeding.

"If we would have just won at least one of our tougher games, we could've have gotten a better seeding," Lainie Douglas said. "That lights a fire under our butt to win a few of these games and make it to the tournament and get a higher seeding."

Missouri State: Lady Bears find defensive rhythm early behind transfer Hannah Linthacum

Marquette has finished above .500 in every season since 2017. The Golden Eagles have also won at least 18 games since that mark, too. They have reached the NCAA Tournament six times in the last 10 years.

It's safe to say this is a big game for the Lady Bears. Not only is it difficult to schedule big-time opponents in today's college basketball landscape, but it's also even harder to host big games.

"The non-conference schedule is a good opportunity to show how talented we are," Douglas said.

Missouri State is off to a flying start with blowout wins over Southeast Missouri State, Wyoming, and Wichita State. The Lady Bears have shown an identity of stifling defense and efficiency inside the lane.

It doesn't matter the opponent, good defense and consistent scoring will always translate to wins.

Marquette's lone loss came against No. 17 Minnesota in an 87-79 defeat. The Lady Bears will also play against the Golden Gophers in the Cancun Challenge on Thursday, Nov. 26.

Missouri State soccer: Bears eliminated from American tournament, awaits postseason bid

While Kaemyn Bekemeier leads the Lady Bears, Marquette's Halle Vice is its star player. The 6-foot-1 senior was an all-Big East First Team member last season. She was also picked to the preseason All-Big East Team list.

Vice has scored at least 12 points in every game, including a 25-point outburst against Minnesota. She can hit deep 3-pointers while scoring inside. She's a true matchup threat for any team she faces.

Missouri State's defense has been spectacular to start. The Lady Bears are allowing just 29% of their opponents to hit shots this season. Interior defense led by Douglas and Hannah Linthacum could be a major key in this game.

A win over Marquette will not only give Missouri State confidence moving forward, but it will also be huge for the team's RPI down the road.

Marquette will be bound to pick up some big wins across the season. The Golden Eagles still have to face Wisconsin and Illinois, along with two games against national championship contender UConn and several other Big East powers.

I Tried Mashed Potatoes From KFC, Popeyes &amp; Jollibee—There's Only One I'd Order Again

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Turns out, a little gravy can make a very big difference.

As a kid who grew up making weekly trips to Boston Market, I’m no stranger to getting mashed potatoes to go. While the chain is a lot harder to find these days, I can still turn to a handful of others when I don’t feel like peeling potatoes and making a batch myself. I might not be rushing to supply my Thanksgiving dinner table with takeout mashed potatoes, but I’m certainly not averse to ordering some to dip my fried chicken into.

I ordered taters from three local fast-food chains to see which one passes muster for texture, seasoning, and all-around flavor. Here’s how it all went down.

Along with Boston Market, I grew up as a regular at KFC. Sadly, its mashed potatoes didn't hit quite as well as I remember. Here, you can order mashed potatoes with or without gravy (the only chain on this list with a gravy-free option), but for this tasting, I opted for the topped version. While they were super creamy, I'd argue they're almost too creamy. They could benefit from a bit more texture and structure. I also found them a bit bland.

I appreciated the gravy, which kept the potatoes from tasting dry, but the potatoes themselves weren't much to write home about.

Popeyes’ mashed potatoes come topped with Cajun gravy, setting them apart from your standard dish. I really enjoyed the flavor of the gravy—it was almost reminiscent of gumbo—and I thought it added a unique element to the mashed potatoes. There was a tiny hint of spice here that also worked really well.

The potatoes are very creamy but thicker than KFC's, and overall, they were more satisfying in terms of both texture and flavor.

Jollibee's taters were quite good and, hands down, the most classic iteration of the bunch. I liked that you could see actual pieces of potato skin in the mashed potatoes. The gravy also tasted the most traditional, with an almost turkey-like flavor. Notably, these were the least creamy potatoes of the three I tried, but thanks to the gravy, they didn't taste dry.

However, the scoop of actual mashed potatoes was pretty meager, and they were drowned in the gravy. Still, I would choose these over the others when I'm looking for that quintessential gravy-and-mashed combo.

A new view of the gamma-ray gap: 14 years of Fermi data reveal around 450 sources in a largely unexplored energy range

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

The universe is filled with light at many different energies. Some of it is invisible to our eyes, including extremely energetic gamma rays, which can only be produced in the most extreme environments in our universe, for example supermassive black holes, exploding stars or astrophysical jets. To "see" gamma rays, astrophysicists use particle experiments in space, such as the Fermi-Large Area Telescope (Fermi-LAT). This instrument was launched into space by NASA in 2008, and it can detect photons from 20 megaelectronvolts (MeV, a unit of energy) to beyond 1 teraelectronvolt (TeV).

“He became a Milan fan” – Buffa claims Chivu changed allegiances in his career

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Today, a suggestion has emerged about Inter coach Cristian Chivu and the fact that he may have been an AC Milan fan in the past.

It’s no secret that lots of people grew up watching Milan as one of the best clubs in the world. Now, kids grow up with Paris Saint-Germain, Real Madrid and Barcelona dominating the world stage. However, once upon a time, that was Milan.

As a result, there will have been people who fell in love with the club due to their days of glory and have stuck around since and one of Inter’s most important figures could be among them.

Lane Kiffin on Arch Manning, Manning family ties before LSU vs. Texas

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

LSU football hosts Texas on Saturday night. For Texas QB Arch Manning, it marks a return to Louisiana. Manning, who played high school football in New Orleans, was the top-ranked recruit in the class of 2023.

Manning comes from a football family. His grandfather, Archie Manning, is an Ole Miss and New Orleans Saints legend. LSU coach Lane Kiffin, who was Ole Miss' head coach from 2020-25, has plenty of connections to the Manning family, from coaching at Archie's alma mater to recruiting Arch out of high school.

Kiffin was asked about his relationship with the Manning family earlier this week.

"If you think about it, I left them twice. You've got to remember the Tennessee part, too, and Peyton, so you probably forgot about that," Kiffin joked.

Kiffin was referencing his exits from Tennessee, where Peyton Manning played, and Ole Miss, both of which came with controversy.

Kiffin said the family has "been awesome, the places I've been, and very supportive while we're there," and that he still talks with them once in a while through text.

after Kiffin left for LSU, Archie Manning recorded a Cameo video for an Ole Miss fan in which Manning called Kiffin a "narcissistic, jerk coach."

The video gained steam on social media, and per Kiffin, Manning apologized.

"He actually left me a message right after apologizing, but he got caught up in the moment after I left and thought it was just a Cameo to the people, and called me a few names," Kiffin said. "But that's okay. I've been called worse, and I'm sure I'll be called the same when we go back to Tennessee, just like Ole Miss."

Kiffin made Arch Manning a priority during his time at Ole Miss. In 2021, he grabbed a "We Want Arch" sign from the crowd before the Rebels' game against Texas A&M and traveled to Baton Rouge to watch Manning and Isidore Newman play Episcopal in the state playoffs.

"I went and saw him play one time … and got to see him just have fun with his teammates," Kiffin said.

Kiffin also saw the other side of Manning's recruitment.

"That's a lot to deal with, even when he was in high school, the expectations and pressure and everything around him," Kiffin said. "When he'd come visit, you kind of see more of the pressure side of what he'd deal with."

Manning chose Texas and Steve Sarkisian, Kiffin's longtime friend and former USC co-worker, over Ole Miss. Saturday will be the first time Kiffin coaches against Manning.

"I really enjoyed the time around Arch. Great kid," Kiffin said. "I think he's an awesome kid, and I hope he does really well, obviously, outside of Saturday."

As Art Week NYC Kicks Off, Galleries Have Millions of Dollars of Art to Sell—And No Shortage of Naras

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Anyone shopping for a Yoshitomo Nara in New York this month has an embarrassment of riches. David Zwirner has filled two Chelsea galleries with the Japanese artist’s paintings. On opening night in Chelsea last week, hours-long lines stretched like highway caravans down 19th and 20th streets, leading to a deluge of posts similarly clogging up the art world’s Instagram feed. The shows have drawings, sculptures, and large new canvases priced as high as $6 million. But bring your checkbook and you might still be out of luck: One source familiar with the exhibition said the largest paintings are being held exclusively for museums. Related Articles Art Week NYC Kicks off at Metropolitan Museum of Art with New York Pols and Art Leaders Only Known Handwritten Draft of Martin Luther King Jr.'s 'I Have a Dream' Speech Could Top $10 M. at Christie's

A few blocks away, Loïc Gouzer’s Fair Warning is offering In the Milky Lake / Thinking One (2011), a monumental painting of a little girl standing waist-deep in pale water, her eyes lowered. Made in the year of the devastating Tōhoku earthquake and tsunami in northeastern Japan, where Nara grew up, the painting has appeared in major retrospectives around the world. It carries a $10–$15 million estimate for Fair Warning’s November 19 auction.

There are more Naras to be had: Sotheby’s is offering Candy Blue Night (2001) at The Now and Contemporary Sale next week with a $5–$7 million estimate, while Christie’s will send Miss Forest / Dark Night (2016), a bronze sculpture estimated at $1 million to $1.5 million, to the block at the 21st Century Evening Sale.

Nara’s auction record of $24.95 million was set at Sotheby’s Hong Kong in 2019. Although he hasn’t come close to $20 million since—at auction, anyways—his largest paintings have continued to sell for roughly $5 million to $15 million, even in a challenging market.

The works on offer this season are hardly interchangeable. In age, scale, medium, and importance, they are all different. But the sheer number of options gives us a snapshot of the art market during New York’s first official Art Week and as the November marquee sales draw near. Collectors can buy from galleries, bid at auction, or wait to see what happens before doing either.

That last option is becoming more and more attractive.

“The market’s healthy, but it continues to be measured with a certain amount of awareness, if not suspicion, about the aggressiveness of private pricing,” Alex Glauber, an art advisor and president of the Association of Professional Art Advisors, told ARTnews.

When collectors find a work at a gallery with a comparable example coming up at auction, Glauber said, more than a few prefer to wait for the result before committing. Unless the opportunity is particularly compelling or fleeting, buyers are taking their time.

It’s an awkward situation for dealers, who have spent months preparing exhibitions that open around the same time collectors flock into town for the evening sales. Art Week NYC, a citywide festival organized by ARTnews, Art in America, and Artforum and running today through Saturday, adds another reason to make the rounds. The galleries have certainly given collectors plenty to look at.

At Gagosian’s West 24th Street spaces, two exhibitions opening just days apart could hardly be more different. Julie Curtiss’s “UNREAL,” which opened Tuesday, gives a compellingly surreal vision of New York life. In Makeover (2026), two women in fur coats and sunglasses—Upper East Side types, if you ask me—wear post-op face masks. In other works, an elephant backs into a bodega, and a couple’s legs appear between washing machines. It’s all a bit naughty, in the best way. The paintings are priced roughly between $100,000 and $300,000, according to two sources familiar with the show.

Curtiss’s auction record of $466,000 was set at Phillips in 2021, amid a white-hot pandemic-era market for wet paint. Her market has cooled considerably since then: according to data from ARTDAI, her top result in the past two years was $115,000, achieved at Sotheby’s in May for a painting similar in size to the one sold in 2021.

Two doors down, on Thursday, Gagosian will open “Meine Schule,” an exhibition of Georg Baselitz‘s final paintings and sculptures. The paintings, priced from around $1.5 million to $2.5 million, include upside-down images of the artist and his wife, Elke, drawn in thin black lines against gold backgrounds, with thick bursts of color. Two others return to his childhood in wartime Germany, showing schoolchildren holding hands beneath a Nazi banner.

We’ve seen this stuff before, but that doesn’t mean it isn’t great. The show’s most affecting work may be Strawinsky (2026), a large, white-patinated bronze figure seated with its head hanging forward and limbs collapsing around its body. The sculpture, priced at around $3 million, grew out of Baselitz’s designs for a marionette production of Stravinsky’s The Soldier’s Tale.

During a walkthrough, Stefan Ratibor, a director with the gallery, remembered visiting Baselitz at his studio outside Munich as the artist, working from a wheelchair, directed the final adjustments to the wooden model. Baselitz signed off on the sculpture and died the following night.

The story lends weight to this show as the last that he selected and designed. It also presents Gagosian with a very different commercial proposition from Curtiss: the final works of a major postwar artist whose output is now finite.

In SoHo, Hauser & Wirth has opened Rashid Johnson‘s “Birthday Boys,” on the heels of his major Guggenheim retrospective last year. Five large mosaics made from ceramic tile, mirrors, oyster shells, and other materials depict fractured, doubled figures. They are priced from $750,000 to $1.2 million, according to a gallery price list I was able to get my hands on. (A similar work from 2021, Untitled Broken Crowd, sold for $1.27 million at Christie’s London in June.) A four-and-a-half-minute film about fathers and sons, which has the awkward, endearing quality of Wes Anderson sans the color saturation or the tweeness, is priced at $200,000.

At Skarstedt, Eric Fischl’s “In Pursuit of Pleasure,” opening Thursday, has 13 paintings of people enjoying, or at least attempting to enjoy, their leisure time. There are dog walkers, sunbathers, and yoga enthusiasts, often looking rather isolated despite the company around them. Two large paintings, Goat Yoga and Polished, Professional, are priced at $800,000 each, while Dog Walker is $425,000. Several other works are already marked reserved on the gallery’s price list.

Sean Kelly, meanwhile, is showing Kehinde Wiley’s portraits of New Yorkers, priced from about $100,000 to $400,000. That range is broadly in line with Wiley’s secondary-market prices, according to ARTDAI, though two outlier results have topped it: $649,000 in 2021 and $838,000 in 2024.

It bears mentioning that this is Wiley’s first show in New York since he was accused of sexual assault in 2024, allegations he has denied, and it may serve as something of a trial balloon for whether New York collectors are ready to embrace him as before. That may also be the thinking behind Christie’s and Sotheby’s decisions to offer Wiley portraits in their upcoming contemporary day sales. The Christie’s work, a double portrait of Nelly Moudime and Najaee Hall, carries an estimate of $150,000 to $200,000, while the Sotheby’s lot, depicting an unnamed male figure, has an estimateof $80,000 to $120,000.

Together, the exhibitions offer collectors a range of possibilities well beyond the eight-figure trophies dominating the auction headlines.

Other galleries are taking a less overtly commercial approach. Almine Rech is presenting James Turrell, Greene Naftali has Lubaina Himid, and White Cube is opening a major installation by Doris Salcedo. At 125 Newbury, Mark Rothko and Adolph Gottlieb are paired in an exhibition examining their Surrealist years.

Perhaps the clearest example is Lévy Gorvy Dayan’s exhibition of Francis Bacon and Willem de Kooning. The show is not selling anything. Instead, it brings together works by two postwar giants, demonstrating the sort of scholarship and access that can attract collectors even when there are no prices to negotiate. Rule No. 1 of sales: Get them in the door.

For all the activity, Glauber thinks the traditional May and November auction seasons have become somewhat less important to primary-market galleries than they were during the market’s hotter years. Auction houses have also backed off their onetime emphasis on emerging artists, which has smoothed the friction that came about when a young artist’s work came to auction while a gallery was still trying to establish primary-market prices.

Still, the basic tension remains. A private sale requires only one willing buyer. A strong auction result generally needs competing bidders, making the public sales an imperfect but useful test of demand.

For collectors considering the Naras scattered across Manhattan, that means plenty of opportunities to compare prices, quality, and availability. For dealers, it means making the case that their work is worth buying now, rather than waiting for the next hammer to fall.

Conan: The Black Pyramid shoves the titular barbarian into a very Hades-inspired action roguelite with the addition of a few other familiar heroes

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

I feel like one thing I only sort of miss from my youth, viewed through the lenses of those rose tinted goggles, is the established intellectual property game that just copies the formula of another, successful game. Obviously there are plenty of genres that are trendy to imitate, but how often do you see something that's just sort of "thing you know slapped on top of game you know?" Perhaps that's a tad mean, but I do genuinely have a fondness for such games, so I'm quite pleased by the announcement of Conan: The Black Pyramid, an action roguelite RPG that so clearly went to Supergiant Games' School of Hades you have to admire the boldness just a little bit.

Conan: The Black Pyramid puts you in the hotseat as the titular barbarian, of course, but also as other characters from the series like Solomon Kane, Dark Agnes, and apparently, more! So, immediately, a notable enough difference from Hades, and one that seems like a neat take. As one of them you then "Descend into eldritch chambers of the Black Pyramid, a mysterious and dangerous place that defies the logic of space and time. Confront the shadows of the champions themselves and piece together the dark secrets of their past."

Where it is similar to Hades lies in the fact that the titular triangle changes in form each time you go in it, and of course there are different abilities and buffs to pick up along the way to aid you in your quest. Each character has their own combination of weapons and abilities, so it does sound like you can mix it up quite nicely. There is, also, 2-player online co-op (sorry to the couch lovers, I suppose), adding to the ways it differs and making it a tad more friendly for those that have exactly one friend who also loves Conan.

Overall, seems like a fresh enough take on Hades while still trying to find some amount of its own identity. And hey, Hades is great, so I'm never upset about having a little bit more of that kind of game. No release date attached to Conan: The Black Pyramid just yet, but it is planned to arrive sometime in 2027. You can check it out on Steam here.

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Nick Wright is not alone in his belief that Clay Travis is faking his right-wing political persona for financial gain. Jemele Hill also accused the OutKick founder for "cosplaying for clicks and views."

Like Wright, Hill has been outspoken about her political beliefs throughout her career, often times making herself a target of right-wing media, including Travis. But the former ESPN on-air personality clearly doesn't believe the Sport & State.com boss believes what he says.

Wright and Travis have been in a back-and-forth on X since yesterday after Wright claimed Travis admitted to him years ago that he espouses conservative views for his personal benefit.

One of my first TV things ever was with Clay, and then we went to dinner after, and he told me he's like, 'You know, I worked for Obama and like, I just, you know, I just do this or whatever,'" Wright said on The Bulwark with Tim Miller. "Luckily there was a third person at this dinner, so if he disputes it, I have a witness."

"I told him, I was like, 'I want to be very clear. If what you're saying is true, that you don't really believe what you're saying, it's just for audience and enrichment, I actually think that's worse, and it is impossible for me to ever respect you,'" Wright continued. "And I left, and that was the last time I ever talked to him. It was 11 years ago."

Travis vehemently denied Wright's claim, but the FS1 broadcaster doubled down, insisting his story was "is 100% true, and verifiable" and offering Travis a $1 million bet to prove he was being honest.

Here is where Hill comes in. The Atlantic journalist quote-tweeted Wright's latest message to Travis, writing that the online firebrand has "been lying about his political beliefs for a long time."

"I'm honestly surprised that people are surprised by this. It's one of the worst kept secrets in our business that dude has been lying about his political beliefs for a long time," Hill said. It's one of the most shameful and dishonest things that one can do. I applaud Nick for keep it real."

As of this writing, Travis has not responded to Hill. Whether he does or not, she's not the only media personality besides Wright to doubt Travis' authenticity.

Bitcoin falls to 12-week low as Jensen Huang’s exit from Nvidia rattles markets

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Huang resigned as the company's president, CEO and board member effective immediately, while MSCI plans to remove bitcoin treasury firm Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell to its lowest level in nearly 12 weeks on Thursday, trading as low as $71,200, as markets reacted to Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's abrupt departure and continued uncertainty surrounding potential selling pressure from MSCI index removals.

Bitcoin was down 3.4% to $71,600 at publication time. Other major crypto assets, including Ethereum, Solana and XRP, also declined more than 3.5% over the past 24 hours.

More than $750 million in crypto positions were liquidated in the past 24 hours, including over $634 million in long positions, according to CoinGlass.

"We remain cautious on BTC as it tests $73k, the level we flagged as first potential support, with no convincing signs of size stepping in at these prices," Jasper De Maere, OTC trader at Wintermute, said Thursday morning. "Some bluechip names are seeing activity, but without large buyers the majors need ETF flows to carry them. That changes if flows turn structurally positive for several days in a row."

Meanwhile, MSCI confirmed it will remove Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30, raising the prospect of selling in the bitcoin proxy. Wintermute described the move as "a passive-selling risk on a BTC proxy where we have no size estimate."

Nvidia's leadership turmoil had been building over the past week. The company launched an investigation into Huang last weekend following reports that he had been a "target of a foreign intelligence effort" amid an alleged affair with a Chinese spy. On Wednesday, the New York Times reported that Huang was also having an affair with a woman who directly reports to him.

On Thursday morning, Huang resigned as president, CEO, and board member, effective immediately. CFO Colette Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor. Kress joined Nvidia in 2013 and will remain CFO during the transition.

President Donald Trump also weighed in, saying former Apple CEO Tim Cook should take the helm of the AI chip developer. "[T]here is only one man to take his place," Trump wrote on Truth Social. "A Legend replaces a Legend!"

Nvidia shares opened Thursday down 12%, extending their decline this month. Nvidia perpetual futures on Hyperliquid generated $2.34 billion in 24-hour trading volume.

$160,000 Hans Arp Sculpture Stolen From Art Cologne

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

“S’élevant (Rising)” (1962) was snatched from Gallery Ludorff's booth on November 8.

German police are seeking answers about the theft of Hans (Jean) Arp's “S’élevant (Rising)” (1962) from Gallery Ludorff's booth at Art Cologne, which went missing from its plinth on the last day of the blue-chip fair on Sunday, November 8, between 4:55pm and 5:10pm.

Since the fair kicked off on November 5, Arp's work had been sitting atop a plinth at the Düsseldorf gallery's booth, sandwiched between two paintings by Ernst Wilhelm Nay. Four employees were at the stand on November 8 and immediately notified the North Rhine-Westphalia State Police upon noticing that the unbolted sculpture, which is valued at €145,000 (~$160,000), had gone missing. As it remains unclear who made off with the 28-centimeter-tall (~11 in) sculpture, police are seeking any information or photographs from visitors or staffers that could aid in the investigation.

Howard Lutnick is next Trump official top Dems want on the chopping block amid NVIDIA scandal

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Congressional Democrats tell Fox News Digital that members of the administration, including Lutnick, Rubio, Homan and Miller could be on their chopping block in the 120th Congress

EXCLUSIVE : Top House Democrats are calling for an investigation into Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over allegations involving NVIDIA's former CEO's reported relationship with an alleged Chinese spy.

NVIDIA CEO Jensen Huang resigned this week following allegations that he had an affair with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed who is suspected of being a Chinese spy.

In the midst of the spy scandal, multiple investigative reports emerged claiming Lutnick received a classified memo warning him about Huang’s relationship with alleged Chinese spy Alice Huang, of no familial relation to the NVIDIA CEO.

"Howard Lutnick needs to be investigated over his role in Jensen Huang and the Chinese spy, we're going to have an investigation into those allegations, that's completely unacceptable," Rep. Ted Lieu, D-Calif., who is expected to become chairman of the House Democratic Caucus, told Fox News Digital.

FOREIGN SPY ‘HONEYTRAPS’ TARGETING AMERICA’S TECH ELITE SPARK ALARM AMID NVIDIA CEO SCANDAL

Howard Lutnick joined the Trump administration as Commerce Secretary in 2025 after co-chairing Trump’s transition team. (Aaron Schwartz/CNP/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

Lieu alleged that despite Lutnick being briefed on the reported relationship between Huang and a woman identified by U.S. intelligence as a Chinese spy, he failed to take action.

Rep. Ro Khanna, D-Calif., a vice chair of the Congressional Progressive Caucus and member of the House Select Committee on China, also expressed support for investigating Lutnick and did not rule out examining Secretary of State Marco Rubio's involvement, as well.

"I think we need transparency," Khanna told Fox News Digital. "I think we need to understand what they knew."

PROGRESSIVE GROUP'S 182-PAGE BLUEPRINT MAPS OUT POTENTIAL DEM INVESTIGATIONS INTO TRUMP, CORPORATE ALLIES

"Rubio knew about the Chinese spy, and then got invited to the China trip," Khanna continued. "We need to get to the bottom of this."

Lutnick and Rubio did not respond to Fox News Digital’s request for comment.

(L-R) US Secretary of the Interior Doug Burgum, US Secretary of State Marco Rubio, US President Donald Trump, US Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth and US Secretary of Commerce Howard Lutnick listen, during a cabinet meeting, in the Cabinet Room of the White House in Washington, DC, of April 30, 2025. (JIM WATSON/AFP via Getty Images)

REBECCA GRANT: JENSEN HUANG OWES AMERICA AN APOLOGY, AND NVIDIA OWES INVESTORS ANSWERS

Many left-leaning lawmakers are jumping on the chance to name the Cabinet official they want to see face scrutiny when Democrats take back control of the House in January.

However, neither Khanna or Lieu shed light on how they would investigate, prosecute or remove Lutnick, and potentially Rubio. Neither of the Cabinet secretaries have been formally accused or indicted of wrongdoing in connection with the NVIDIA case.

House Minority Leader Rep. Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., who is currently running unopposed for the Democratic nomination for Speaker of the House, did not respond to Fox News Digital’s request for comment on whether he spoke to either lawmakers on their desires to take action against Lutnick and Rubio.

HAKEEM JEFFRIES DECLARES BID FOR HOUSE SPEAKER AFTER GOP'S MIDTERM MASSACRE

The lawmakers' remarks signal that congressional Democrats could use their incoming majority to investigate senior Trump administration officials over their handling of alleged Chinese intelligence threats.

Rep. Ro Khanna (D-CA) appears on "Meet the Press" in Washington D.C., Sunday, Sep. 1, 2024 (William B. Plowman/NBC via Getty Images)

It is unclear whether Republican lawmakers will support the Democrats' investigations. But following Huang’s resignation, House Select Committee on China Chairman John Moolenaar, R-Mich., announced that the outgoing CEO has agreed to brief congressional committees following the scandal.

He also advocated for Congress to act quickly to pass bipartisan export control legislation in the year-end national defense bill.

Moolenaar welcomed the resignation and added that companies such as NVIDIA should "never jeopardize our nation’s security by giving Chinese companies access to their most advanced chips," and called on them to "change its corporate practices and appoint leadership that makes national security its top priority."

Napoli, quale le priorità del mercato di Manna secondo gli opinionisti

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il Napoli pensa al mercato di riparazione. Ma quali devono essere gli obiettivi principali per migliorare questa rosa? Ecco cosa hanno detto gli opinionisti della radio di Tuttomercatoweb.com.

Stefano Impallomeni: "Il Napoli ha 15 punti in classifica dopo 11 giornate, una media bassa. Allegri accetta il Napoli perché pensa, al netto di un non mercato, sia comunque forte. Oggi tutti a dire che il mercato va fatto. Io credo che c'è nello sport chi arriva primo e chi arriva indietro. Quindi che mercato deve fare? Anche se gli compri Pelè a questa squadra è difficile che recuperi per fare tanti punti".

Franco Ordine: "Le priorità sono due: un attaccante che dia il cambio a Hojlund e recuperare gli infortunati, perché con la rosa in salute, Allegri è uno che porta la nave in porto".

Giocondo Martorelli: "Il problema è che il Napoli non ha fatto mercato in estate. Credo dovrebbe intervenire in tanti ruoli, dovrebbe svecchiare ora e finire la prossima estate. Dovrà trovare giocatori giovani e in tutti i ruoli. Deve cercare di rifondare, c'è tanto da lavorare".

Enzo Bucchioni: "Siamo a fine ciclo, dovevi affrontare la situazione prima. E' stato preso Allegri perrivitalizzare un gruppo, che però non ha fatto nulla. Sono giocatori che non possono essere più motivati, hanno già dato. E a questo punto che fai a gennaio? Ai nomi che ci sono non corrisponde la realtà. Conte aveva capito tutto, ci sono giocatori spremuti che non hanno più voglia. ADL deve decidere se puntellare la squadra, prendendo 2-3 punti, o fare altro".

Fulvio Giuliani: "Serve un attaccante come il pane. Lasciamo stare Lucca, Hojlund lo stai spremendo e non è un bomber. Ad oggi sei più vicino al terzultimo posto che al quarto. Oggi chiunque così sarebbe stato accompagnato alla porta, Allegri neanche viene discusso. Tra i tanti errori c'è quello di aver dato carta bianca ad Allegri, che non è interessato ai destini della squadra".

Donato Di Campli: "eve iniziare a programmare un ricambio generazionale. E' una squadra vecchia e spremuta. La stagione è fallimentare e lo si poteva prevedere da inizio anno, prima dell'arrivo di Allegri. Manna deve rifondare la squadra nei punti fondamentali. Ci sono giocatori che hanno contratti onerosi e non si muoveranno da Napoli se non dopo aver trovato un'adeguata situazione. Ed è un problema per Manna. a gennaio ADL deve mettersi in testa che deve mettere denaro, puntellando i reparti dove sta soffrendo, vedi la difesa".

Ivan Zazzaroni: "ADL dovrà prima mandare via qualcuno, deve farlo per forza. La posizione di Allegri non è mai stata traballante. Quando vedo il Napoli, vedo degli errori tecnici dei giocatori. Li fanno perché sono scarsi".

Alberto Polverosi: "Allegri ha bisogno di un centravanti vero. Poi ha bisogno di qualcosa a centrocampo, anche se Allegri ha arretrato De Bruyne e gli ha portato dei vantaggi sul piano della manovra".

Stefan Schwoch: "La necessità primaria è trovare un attaccante, che possa sostituire Hojlund, perché Lucca ha fatto male e il rapporto è diventato pesante, così come con Lang. Credo che il Napoli debba trovare una punta e ringiovanire la rosa. E quando hai tanti impegni questo pesi. Credo Manna stia lavorando in questa direzione".

English Soccer Star Jude Bellingham Reveals He Can’t Drive or Cook

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Jude Bellingham, star of England’s men’s soccer team and Real Madrid, admitted his skills are limited to the pitch, and that he hasn’t yet learned how to drive or cook.

Jude Bellingham may be one of the greatest footballers of his generation.

But as it turns out, Team England’s star midfielder’s basic everyday skills—or lack thereof—help level the playing field, bringing him closer to us mere mortals.

“There’s loads of things I don’t know how to do,” Bellingham told British Vogue in an interview published Nov. 10. “I can’t cook, I can’t drive. I’m good at what I do, but everything else? Not that great. I rely on my friends, my family to help me do stuff—chef, cleaner, mum, whoever else. That’s where I’m at.”

In fact, he’s been so busy keeping his eye on the literal ball—and doing a really good job at it—he may have lost track of life’s other skills.

“I work hard every day and all day, and then when I get home,” the 23-year-old said, “I’ve probably neglected learning how to do some of the more basic things.”

In his defense, not only is learning those life skills par for the course for many early twenty-somethings, but Bellingham’s daytime job also takes up a lot of his time.

“I feel mature in a football context because I’ve played nearly 400 games,” he reflected. “But in a life context I don’t feel that mature now, to be honest.”

But, as he noted, “There’ll be a time and a place for that, I’m sure.”

In the meantime, he’s focused on his skills on the pitch, where Bellingham’s natural talents already having brough him to the World Cup twice, in 2022 and 2026. And in their most recent outing, England finished third—the country’s best result since 1966.

“The World Cup, for me, was probably the best experience I’ve had in my life, full stop,” the Real Madrid player said. “While I was in it, I really made the conscious decision to enjoy this.”

After all, “It doesn’t come around very often,” he added. “It’s rare.”

And while Bellingham’s World Cup performance won him fans around the world—as did his endearing bromance with former Borussia Dortmund teammate and Norway’s star Erling Haaland—nowhere was more grateful for his performance than England.

As he noted, “I’ve seen a lot of people say, ‘I don’t think I’ve ever had a night when I was so happy and so proud to be English.’”

For a look back at the many celebrities who tuned in live to see Bellingham’s athletic prowess shine on the pitch, read on.

What’s Going on With Katseye?

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Katseye started the year off strong, but the past few months have been marred with hiccups. What’s going on?

In February, the six members of the group delivered a spirited performance of “Gnarly” during the Best New Artist medley at the 2026 Grammy Awards. In April, they made their Coachella debut on the heels of their single “Pinky Up.” By then, they were down to five members, with Manon Bannerman on an indefinite hiatus to focus on her “health and wellbeing,” a break that is currently in its ninth month.

“I’m really grateful for the patience and kindness everyone has shown during this time,” Bannerman said in April, adding that she’d had “positive conversations” with the group’s labels. “I feel supported. I’m happy and I’m healthy.” Katseye powered full-speed ahead in the meantime.

Over the summer, the group announced their Wildworld Tour, a global trek scheduled to put them on arena stages in the fall. Leading up to the launch, Katseye released their third EP, Wild, in August and made a major push to expand their audience with an appearance as musical guest on Saturday Night Live in September.

They were now down to four members, with Sophia Laforteza on a hiatus expected to conclude next January. “Making this decision wasn’t easy, but I’m learning that health has to come first,” she said over the summer. “I’m realizing that if I don’t take care of my mind and body now, I won’t be able to keep doing what I love the most for so long.”

And so Katseye began the North American leg of the tour as a quartet. But last week, their Detroit concert was postponed shortly before they were scheduled to hit the stage. “Megan [Skiendiel] is unwell and unable to perform tonight, and we want to make sure she has the time she needs to rest and recover,” the group shared on Instagram. Two more postponements followed with the confirmation that Skiendiel’s previously undisclosed illness was actually a concussion. This came on top of her sprained ankle, which resulted in the cancellation of their appearance at Olivia Rodrigo’s Daisy Chain Fields Festival in August. Trending Stories Sade Are Not Attending Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Bob Dylan’s Meeting With Pope Leo: People Fixated on the Hood, But the Hands Spoke Volumes Megan Thee Stallion and Daniel Radcliffe Reunite for Season Two of ‘Reggie Dinkins’ Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

On Nov. 10, the four remaining active members of Katseye returned to the stage for the Austin stop on the Wildworld Tour. Daniela Avanzini, Lara Raj, and Yoonchae Jeung performed as usual, and Skiendiel performed on a chair while wearing blacked-out sunglasses, likely to block the light and avoid any further discomfort related to the concussion. Skiendiel prefaced ahead of the Nov. 10 show that she would be performing on a “modified schedule,” which included reduced choreography and necessary breaks.

On Nov. 11, Katseye performed in Dallas with all four members back on their feet, though Skiendiel continued to wear her sunglasses throughout the show. With three dates already postponed, the group still has nine more to go on the North American leg of the Wildworld Tour. In January, after a brief two-month break, Katseye will resume the tour with stops across Asia and Australia. By then, Laforteza will have returned from her mental health break. We’ll have to stay tuned to see what happens next.

Warriors reveal Stephen Curry injury diagnosis after MRI on abdomen

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Golden State Warriors were already without one star. Now they'll be without their Most Valuable Player candidate, Stephen Curry, for the short-term future, too.

The Warriors announced Thursday, Nov. 12 that an MRI confirmed Curry suffered an oblique abdominal tear and added that he would be reevaluated in two weeks. The injury took place Wednesday, Nov. 11 in an eventual 115-108 loss against the Houston Rockets.

Curry walked off the floor midway through the first quarter, clearly grimacing and favoring his right side.

The sequence took place just 5:35 into the game. Curry had scored 3 points on 1-of-3 shooting by that point. He did not return to the floor for the rest of the first quarter, and he was not back at the bench area to start the second period.

ANALYSIS: What does Curry injury mean for Warriors? Can they weather the storm?

The Warriors announced minutes into the second quarter that Curry would not return for the rest of the game with right abdominal soreness.

After the game, Warriors coach Steve Kerr said that Curry said he "tweaked something in his abdominal area," which Curry told Kerr he had never done before. Kerr added that "concern is high" though he reiterated that he had no idea of the extent of the injury.

During the game, Curry did have a collision with Rockets forward Amen Thompson earlier in the game, when Curry had driven to the hoop on an attempted layup. Then, Curry had been fighting off a screen against Rockets center Alperen Şengün. As soon as that sequence ended, Curry once again grabbed his side.

Finally, just moments before he left the game, Curry curled around a screen and put up a 3-point shot. As soon as he landed and stepped backwards, he grabbed at his right side, near his hip.

Curry had been in the midst of a renaissance and had been elevating the play of the Warriors, who entered Wednesday night 8-3, which was tied for the third best record in the Western Conference.

Prior to suffering the injury, Curry was averaging 30.6 points, 4.1 rebounds and 4.3 assists per game in nine appearances. He was converting 3-pointers at a blistering 45.7%, which would mark a career-high. His scoring total entering Wednesday night had ranked second in the NBA, behind only Luka Dončić's historic 39.3 points per game, for all players with at least five games played.

The Warriors were already without Jimmy Butler, who has missed the start of the season as he rehabs the torn anterior cruciate ligament he suffered in January 2026.

You're probably aware by now of the scandal at Nvidia. Outgoing CEO Jensen Huang has announced his departure following allegations of two extramarital affairs within the last week. This is reverberating through the tech and games industries, causing Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney to weigh in.

He did so on Twitter, where he expressed considerable respect for Huang. "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity," he writes. "Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction."

In the interest of my own sanity, I'm going to gloss over anyone calling the transition of CEOs anywhere a "sad day for humanity." Nonetheless, Sweeney continued tweeting about the matter.

A sad day for NVIDIA, developers, America, and all humanity. Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction.November 12, 2026

"It’s terribly sad and, in my opinion, far too much government heavy-handedness - including through leaks and anonymous sourcing to journalists with dubious motives - no excuse for abandoning due process," he says, presumably in reference to the reporting and current internal investigations going on surrounding the alleged impropriety.

The government involvement stems from the belief one of Huang's relationships was with a potential Chinese operative. Information is limited at present, and is likely to remain so for the foreseeable future.

"Look at all of the free passes government has given to Apple in its efforts to monopolize app distribution and payments - including cover for lawbreaking here and in allied nations overseas," Sweeney adds in another follow-up tweet. "Selective enforcement and opaque dealing are antithetical to a free nation."

His first post referenced a tweet he made in 2024, celebrating Huang's position at the forefront of Nvidia and highlighting how closely Epic and the hardware manufacturer have worked in the past, particularly through the Unreal Engine. How that business agreement continues is something that'll need to be worked out with Huang's successor.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

Chief financial officer and executive vice president Colette Kress is now interim CEO, while Nvidia shareholders begin the process of finding his replacement.

Nvidia firing CEO Jensen Huang "would be an Apple firing Steve Jobs level of idiocy," Epic Games boss Tim Sweeney says

He previously served as Senior Anime Writer at Dexerto and News Editor at The Digital Fix, on top of providing work for Variety, IGN, Den of Geek, PC Gamer, and many more. Besides Studio Ghibli, horror movies, and The Muppets, he enjoys action-RPGs, heavy metal, and pro-wrestling. He interviewed Animal once, not that he won’t stop going on about it or anything.

The Saga of Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s Fake Leather Jacket

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The internet loved his outrageous new look. There was just one problem.

There are several ways to respond when you've admitted to an affair, your company's shares are tumbling, and even the White House is discussing your private life. Turning up in a spectacularly studded Saint Laurent leather jacket is one of them. Resigning is another.

For a while, it seemed like Jensen Huang, the 63-year-old former CEO of Nvidia, had opted for the former. A photograph showing the tech billionaire in an archival Hedi Slimane YSL biker jacket went viral this week. The internet was delighted. There was just one problem: The photograph was fake.

The resignation, which came just this morning, was real.

As CNN reported, the image had been doctored from a genuine photograph taken at a Seoul airport in June. Nvidia, which one might imagine has other things to deal with right now, confirmed it wasn't real.

But perhaps the reason so many people believed it was that Huang already dresses like this. Leather jackets are his uniform. He reportedly owns 30 or 40 of them, including a snazzy reptile-effect number worn to deliver his company's keynote at the Consumer Electronics Show.

And Hedi Slimane was the perfect designer to bring into the fantasy. The Frenchman, who ran Saint Laurent from 2012 to 2016, made his name dressing skinny rock stars and turning their disheveled, devil-may-care attitude—later rebranded as so-called “indie sleaze”—into expensive luxury fashion. His leather jackets were practically a uniform for men who were determined to look as though they didn't care what anyone thought and/or having a midlife crisis.

For a billionaire in the middle of a spectacularly public scandal, it was quite a look. One Instagram commenter described Huang as the first person to achieve "negative aura" in archival Hedi.

There's another irony. Huang has previously said his wife Lori—i.e., the wronged party in all this—helps choose his clothes.

And then there's the technology itself. Nvidia makes the chips that power the AI revolution, including the tools capable of ... producing convincing fake photographs.

In conclusion then, the image wasn't real. But as a portrait of a billionaire in the middle of a spectacularly public crisis, we so wanted it to be.
