Fantasy Football Rankings: Hayden Winks' WR Blueprint for Week 11

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Blueprint is my weekly column and basically everything that I use to build out the most accurate rankings I can. There will be this WR column, an RB column and a combination column on the onesie positions. You will find film notes and endless charts. Maybe enough charts to stress test the Yahoo website. I kid.

It's impossible to get every ranking thoroughly early in the week, so be sure to keep checking in on these columns through Sunday morning. As always, my full rankings at every position are available.

1. Jaxon Smith-Njigba (at LV) - 90.5 proj. receiving yards

JSN has played four games with Drew Lock over the past two years, and it's essentially been exactly the same as the other games. He averages 9.5 targets and 106 receiving YPG. Meanwhile, the Raiders have been gashed by downfield targets of late. Jordan Watkins was the WR11 on a deep touchdown, while AD Mitchell (WR3) and Keon Coleman (WR15) also got home with long scores. This is a beautiful spot for JSN.

2. Ja'Marr Chase (vs. PIT) - 89.5 proj. receiving yards

Chase's first half last week was one of the best ever, in terms of all the different ways he can win. He's back to being the WR1 on WR7 usage this month in the league's most pass-heavy offense in neutral situations (66%). The big games will continue, to the point that he'd easily be a top-five overall pick if drafting today.

Chase has faced the Steelers countless times. He's finished as the WR5, WR1, WR5 and WR11 against them since 2024, removing one game with Joe Flacco. Pittsburgh has been excellent against fantasy WRs of late against an easy schedule, but this is a totally different beast. The Bengals have a 26.5-point team total (fifth) as 3.5-point home favorites, and offenses facing the Steelers have the highest neutral pass rate allowed in neutral situations (60%), too.

3. CeeDee Lamb (vs. SF) - 89.5 proj. receiving yards

Lamb dominated without George Pickens last week, bringing him up to WR3 on WR2 usage this season. He's averaging 100.4 receiving YPG, while the Cowboys OL is back to looking dominant.

The 49ers defense has been excellent, and they added a couple of star players of late in DeForest Buckner and Nick Bosa. A game total of 55.5 points is high enough to ignore any defensive stats here, though. Dallas has a strong 26.0-point team total (sixth) as field-goal dogs.

4. Amon-Ra St. Brown (vs. NE) - 78.5 proj. receiving yards

It's been a fairly quiet season for St. Brown, who is the WR7 on WR3 usage. He's a positive regression candidate, as he hasn't scored since late September. He still has a 37% target share over the past month despite the forgettable box scores, and the Lions score touchdowns, last time I checked. Good times are coming.

This week brings an enticing draw against a Patriots defense that ranks third in half PPR points allowed to WRs. Star CB Christian Gonzalez will likely see more of Jameson Williams on the boundary than St. Brown on the inside. Malik Washington (WR17), Luther Burden III (WR6) and Omar Cooper Jr. (WR6) are similar players who have played well against them this past month. Detroit enters as 2.5-point home favorites with a 27-point team total (fourth) in Germany.

5. Tee Higgins (vs. PIT) - 76.5 proj. receiving yards

Higgins is the WR4 on WR13 usage this season. He's dunking on everyone and can rack up targets when the Bengals are pushed, as they have the highest neutral pass rate (66%) and are struggling to run the ball in short yardage.

The Steelers have the highest neutral pass rate (60%) allowed on defense, and Higgins has a strong history against them. He's been the WR8, WR15, WR12, WR16, WR9 and WR4 since 2022, removing the lone Week 18 game. That's as good as it gets. Cincinnati enters as 3.5-point home favorites with an implied team total of 26.5 points (fifth).

6. Chris Olave (vs. CAR) - 74.5 proj. receiving yards

Olave came out of the bye with a solid game on 12 targets. The Saints welcomed back LT Kelvin Banks Jr. and introduced first-round WR Jordyn Tyson. Tyler Shough also had another week to rest his fractured left hand. It won't be a surprise if the Saints bounce back after a poor October due to injuries and matchups. Olave is the WR10 on WR7 usage this season.

The Panthers defense has been pathetic of late, while missing EDGE Jaelan Phillips and both of their starting CBs. We'll see if they return here. Olave has battled Carolina throughout his career and won both matchups against it last year, finishing as the WR10 and WR6 with 85-1 and 104-1 lines, respectively. New Orleans is listed as 2.5-point home favorites with an implied total of 25.5 points (seventh). That's probably higher than you thought.

7. Christian Watson (vs. MIN) - 73.5 proj. receiving yards

Watson continues to look like a top-10 NFL WR on tape, and he's produced like that in fantasy (WR5 on WR12 usage). Jordan Love is playing the sport unlike anyone else, and it's been the best TV going this fall.

A matchup with the Vikings will be so entertaining. Watson shredded Minnesota for 147-2 (WR2) back in Week 1, and I'd argue the Packers are better suited to handle this unique defense now than they were before, given the strides the OL has taken. Green Bay has also faced Brian Flores more than anyone else, so it's less of a surprise.

Beyond that, Love has faced the most blitzes and is the QB1 in yards and touchdowns against it, on a QB2 PFF grade. Watson is the WR1 in yards against the blitz himself, and his target share is at 18% without the blitz and 31% against the blitz. That is a very notable gap.

8. Justin Jefferson (at GB) - 77.5 proj. receiving yards

I wrote a ton about Kirk Cousins and Jefferson here.

As for the matchup, Jefferson had his best game of the year against the Packers back in Week 1, finishing as the WR3 with a 92-2 receiving line. Green Bay slowed down the Panthers duo, the Cowboys duo and Amon-Ra St. Brown this month, however. It's a strong defense that is well coached and has the best safety group across the league. I do trust Kirk Cousins to figure it out, assuming he can start right away.

9. Malik Nabers (vs. WAS) - 66.5 proj. receiving yards

Nabers is the WR7 on WR3 usage this month after ramping up in September. He's the WR2 in targets since then and, aside from a dropped touchdown last week, he's been outstanding on tape. J.J. McCarthy has been better than expected and has been willing to launch the ball when in doubt. I still don't trust his play-to-play consistency, but the pass game is largely just Nabers and Isaiah Likely.

The matchup is fantastic with the Commanders struggling in the secondary and placing two DTs on injured reserve. They've allowed the fourth-most passing yards per game (256) on the third-highest neutral pass rate (56%). Washington just let Puka Nacua do whatever he needed last week en route to a WR2 overall finish. Nabers had 121 yards and a score (WR4) against them in Week 5, too. The Giants enter as 2.5-point home favorites carrying a 22-point team total (16th). This is a great spot on paper. I just hope McCarthy doesn't get flustered by the chaos new DC Daronte Jones can bring.

10. Parker Washington (at TEN) - 62.5 proj. receiving yards

Washington's week-to-week swing is very frustrating, but it's not because of his role or play. The Jaguars have simply been blowing teams out to a degree that's kept Washington without the ball late. He's averaging 4.5 targets in the first half (48 YPG) versus 2.4 targets in the second half (13 YPG), solely because the Jaguars are leading in point differential (+108) by a substantial 29 points. Washington can be the reason why Jacksonville is blowing a team out, but if not, then the targets will be forgettable. He's the WR27 in targets per game. Washington is a high-ceiling fantasy WR2.

This is an eruption spot in every way. The Titans have allowed the most fantasy points to WRs (33.0) on the second-most passing yards per game (273). Where they've been lit up the most is underneath and intermediate, where Washington does a bulk of his damage. That'll be an even bigger issue without superstar DT Jeffery Simmons and S Amani Hooker. Jacksonville enters as 10.5-point road favorites with an implied team total of 28.5 points (third).

11. Drake London (vs. KC) - 72.5 proj. receiving yards

London has finished as the WR31 in all Michael Penix Jr. games, aside from the monsoon disaster in Tampa Bay in Week 8. In all career games with Penix, London is averaging 93.4 YPG on 9.8 targets as a clear-cut fantasy WR1. Even during this down month, London has held a 25% target share. I'd buy any hypothetical dip with the Falcons passing significantly more recently, potentially because Brian Robinson Jr. is on injured reserve.

There's no spin-zoning this. The matchup with the Chiefs is terrible if CB1 Mansoor Delane (shoulder) suits up. Even without him, CB2 Nohl Williams is a high-quality player in addition to how well-coached this defense is under DC Steve Spagnuolo. Teams facing Kansas City have the third-lowest neutral pass rate (46%), though they often have to pass more in the final quarter due to game script. The Falcons are 3.5-point home underdogs with an implied total of 21.0 points (18th).

12. Matthew Golden (vs. MIN) - 69.5 proj. receiving yards

Golden reminds me of a young Stefon Diggs. What a talent and just 23 years old without a ton of experience dating back to college and his rookie season. Whew.

The matchup is fascinating with the Vikings. Golden was the WR20 on WR2 usage against them in Week 1, but as I wrote in the Christian Watson blurb, the Packers are better suited to beat the blitz than everyone else. Golden's target share is at 29% against the blitz and without the blitz.

13. Zay Flowers (vs. LAC) - 69.5 proj. receiving yards

It's simply best to throw out the past two games with Joe Fagnano, as Jacoby Brissett is light-years better. As a baseline, Flowers is averaging 12.3 half PPR points and 66 YPG in nine games since last year with Tyler Huntley, who is probably a full tier worse than Brissett himself. Flowers will struggle to get all the way back to the league-leading yards per route metrics that he had in September, but he can sneak back into the fantasy WR1 mix once Brissett gets his feet wet again. It's helpful that the Ravens have first-round LG Vega Ioane and C Cam Jurgens in the lineup. I'm buying the dip.

The matchup with the winless Chargers is strong. They've allowed the fifth-most points against fantasy WRs and were especially shredded by the Texans and Rams over the past two games with S Derwin James on IR. Los Angeles also traded away LB1 Daiyan Henley this week. The Ravens are 4.5-point home favorites with an implied team total of 21.5 points (17th).

14. DJ Moore (at NYJ) - 45.5 proj. receiving yards

Moore has played two normal games to me: Week 1 (WR8 on WR23 usage) and Week 8 (WR13 on WR9 usage). Everything else has been derailed by injury and a matchup with the Vikings' chaotic defense. We're still fully in limbo, but the ceiling is very obvious. I think the type of routes Moore is running suits his skill set best. He's a great fantasy trade deadline target.

This is an eruption spot here. Buffalo is favored on the road as an 8.5-point favorite with an implied team total of 29.5 points (first). The Jets have had a very easy fantasy WR schedule this season, yet have recently allowed WR20, WR8 and WR10 finishes this month to Jalen Nailor, Caleb Douglas and Pop Douglas, respectively.

We'll make sure he plays after missing last week. It's a big-time matchup if he can, with the game total sitting at a wonderful 55.5 points.

16. Tetairoa McMillan (at NO) - 66.5 proj. receiving yards

McMillan is averaging 59 YPG with Jalen Coker and 92 YPG without him on 2.0 fewer targets per game, dating back to last year. He's down to WR36 on WR20 usage this month with Coker back in the lineup and Bryce Young coming back to earth behind an injured OL. Hopefully LG Damien Lewis can return here. McMillan is far closer to the WR2/3 border over a big sample.

That said, this matchup is outstanding. The Saints sit 16th in half PPR points allowed per game to WR rooms, but they have been vulnerable downfield by giving up the third-most half PPR points per game on passes over 15 air yards. Did you see the Browns WRs last week? McMillan brings downfield usage with 18 targets over 15 air yards this season, supported by Carolina's top-3 neutral pass rate of 60%. The Panthers are listed as 2.5-point road underdogs with an implied team total of 23 points (12th).

17. Nico Collins (at CLE) - 55.5 proj. receiving yards

Collins not being a full-time player still boggles the mind, but expectations need to be reset in terms of his weekly floor. He's the WR53 on WR50 usage over the past month, even though C.J. Stroud has been playing out of his mind. Collins has averaged a 17% target share on 5.3 targets per game during this bizarre month stretch. The Texans will keep rotating dirty-work Xavier Hutchinson, explosive threat Tank Dell, whatever we're calling Marvin Harrison Jr. and even deep threat Kayshon Boutte. The three saving graces are Collins is amazing, Stroud is firmly a top-10 QB on film and the Texans are now sixth in neutral pass rate since Week 4 when Collins returned.

The Browns are the only defense that plays man coverage at a notable rate. They're talented in the secondary, specifically with CB1 Denzel Ward, but league-wide trends against man coverage lean WR while zone means a higher target share to RBs/TEs. Collins hasn't had the best season against man coverage (1.3 YPRR on 21% targets per route), but he has been amongst the best against it over the past few seasons. Houston heads to Cleveland as 4.5-point road favorites with an implied team total of 22 points (15th).

18. Jameson Williams (vs. NE) - 56.5 proj. receiving yards

The best Jamo advice is to stay within the WR15 and WR25 every week, whether he's played well or not. He's the WR20 on WR33 usage this season, with three games inside the top 12 at the position.

This week's matchup is screaming Yahoo purple after the Patriots were shredded by the Packers' downfield duo last week. Williams will have to do it again with star CB1 Christian Gonzalez in his back pocket.

19. Garrett Wilson (vs. BUF) - 61.5 proj. receiving yards

Wilson should be back to a full-time role from his concussion this week. His issue is that the entire skill group is healthy now, with both WRs, both TEs and both RBs in the lineup, and there are only so many points available to go around. Over his last four games, Wilson is the WR28 on WR18 usage on a 27% target share. The Jets will run the ball plenty and have struggled to get off the field on the other side of the ball. This season, the Jets' WR room is 24th in usage, averaging 23.1 expected half PPR points. It's harder to make the math work with Omar Cooper Jr. playing well and AD Mitchell getting his own deep/red zone looks.

The Bills DL is pretty good, but their secondary has been a problem beyond CB1 Christian Benford, who has been battling injuries throughout the year. They added S Amani Hooker at the deadline to help out. To date, they've allowed the seventh-most half PPR points to WRs on the sixth-highest neutral pass rate (55%). The Jets are 8.5-point home underdogs carrying an implied team total of 21 points (20th). For what it's worth, Wilson has been the WR60, WR25 and WR2, respectively, against the Bills in his most recent games.

20. Rashee Rice (at ATL) - 54.5 proj. receiving yards

We'll learn more about Xavier Worthy and Josh Downs later in the week.

Just like last week against the Jets, Rice has another great matchup that fits his underneath skill set. The Falcons have allowed the fifth-fewest fantasy points per game on throws under 10 air yards this season, and they just traded away S Jesse Bates. The Chiefs project for 24.75 points (eighth).

Brown is traveling to Germany, and it's not for platelet-rich therapies. Nope, the NFL is sending more teams overseas to play football games. Coach Mike Vrabel said that anyone traveling does not mean they're assured to play. More later.

22. Jalen Coker (at NO) - 46.5 proj. receiving yards

Coker has cooled off. He's only the WR60 on WR45 usage since returning, hauling in just 3.3 receptions per game over the past month. This will likely be his worst stretch of the year based on how good he was in 2025 and before his injury this year. Coker is too good on tape to be this unproductive.

The matchup is a strong one, as Coker can play intermediate and downfield enough to take advantage of where the Saints have been beaten most frequently. New Orleans is allowing the fifth-most passing yards per game (256), and the Panthers are projected for 23.0 points (12th) in a dome environment. Coker had 60-1 against the Saints late last year during his breakout.

23. Germie Bernard (at CIN) - 48.5 proj. receiving yards

Bernard has averaged 7.6 targets per game without Michael Pittman Jr. as the new slot/flanker hybrid. He's the underneath WR, while DK Metcalf runs clear-out routes. Bernard is a post-bye rookie bump candidate now that Aaron Rodgers has welcomed him to the trust tree. Bernard has been the WR26 on WR26 usage this month, racking up WR10 receptions per game.

The matchup with Cincy can be difficult for boundary WRs but CB1 D.J. Turner will be busy with Metcalf, while Bernard gets the easier matchups. The Bengals' LB and S groups have been very poor this year, particularly with tackling and Bernard is not easy to bring down. They've allowed the 10th-most points to WRs in the 5-15 air yards range. A lively 23.0-point team total (14th) as 3.5-point dogs sets Bernard up well.

24. Stefon Diggs (at NYG) - 38.5 proj. receiving yards

Diggs is the WR23 on WR26 usage this year, and his expected half PPR points have bounced between 7.8 and 12.6 in all but one game. Last week's five targets were the season-low outlier, but nothing material has changed. He's the part-time slot-only WR with Antonio Williams, Treylon Burks and Van Jefferson rotating in on the perimeter. Diggs is a step slower this year but remains very crafty and a focal point of OC David Blough's designs in a very mild version of the ARSB role.

One of the biggest NFL stories has been the Giants secondary taking a major leap. They've allowed the third-fewest passing YPG (189), partially because CB1 Deonte Banks has turned into a man-coverage monster. Now, Diggs will be shielded from him while playing inside, but the Commanders only have a 19.5-point team total (22nd) because this defense has stepped up. Diggs didn't suit up for the Week 5 contest against them.

25. Deebo Samuel Sr. (at DAL) - 38.5 proj. receiving yards

We'll learn more about who is playing later in the week, but Samuel was at least a limited participant on Wednesday and Mike Evans is unlikely to play. Samuel should be a part-time player if active, yet he was still fantasy viable in that role before his injury with WR13, WR17, WR61 and WR14 finishes on WR45 usage in September.

The team total sits at 29.5 points (second), and the Cowboys have allowed the third-most fantasy points to WRs on throws under 10 air yards, where Samuel does most of his damage. It's an amazing spot if he can get in a full practice by Friday.

26. Denzel Boston (vs. HOU) - 47.5 proj. receiving yards

Boston is the WR32 on WR27 usage across the entire season. His role will come with volatility, as he's the primary downfield and red-zone threat and those passes are more difficult for bad QBs to complete. The good news is Boston looks like a real deal top-20 WR in the making as a diet-Tee Higgins. The Browns have targeted him the most in second halves, in the red zone and against single high coverage, so being projected to lose is actually good news for Boston.

The Texans have elite edge rushers and good (but volatile) corners. CB2 Kamari Lassiter has been dunked on for a chunk of the season, though some of their leaks in zone have tightened up over the past month. They've still allowed the 10th-most fantasy points to WRs this month and the second-most across the entire season. Houston has allowed the fourth-most points on targets over 15 air yards, where Boston has made many highlight plays. Cleveland has a 17.5-point team total, however, so all of this data needs to be taken with a major grain of salt. Everyone expects Houston to play much better than it has moving forward.

27. KC Concepcion (vs. HOU) - 49.5 proj. receiving yards

Concepcion is up to WR20 on WR31 usage this month after a fairly slow start to his rookie campaign. His juice after the catch is evident and he can stretch the field, even as a slot hybrid player. He reminds me of a young Zay Flowers. Concepcion has been their primary screen player and top target in first halves when coach Todd Monken is most in his bag. There is still no such thing as a floor in this offense, but it wouldn't be surprising if Concepcion leads the team in targets moving forward. He looks like a Round 1 talent to me.

The Texans defense has been better of late, yet still extremely disappointing across the season. I still don't view this as an easy matchup despite all of the production allowed, but where I can be sold is that it's the easiest inside, where SCB Jalen Pitre has been pretty rough and SS Reed Blankenship has been out. Cleveland is slated as a 4.5-point home underdog with an implied team total of 17.5 points (24th).

28. DK Metcalf (at CIN) - 49.5 proj. receiving yards

Metcalf doesn't have a top-10 finish yet. He peaked as the WR12 in Week 6 and has been the WR48 on average, despite being 14th in targets per game. Fourteenth!!! He just hasn't been good in a far more vertical role, with Germie Bernard taking over the underneath role in the slot/flanker spot.

The Bengals have allowed the fewest fantasy points to WRs on throws beyond 15 air yards, where Metcalf is seeing a bulk of his looks. He'll battle elite athlete CB1 DJ Turner, while the rest of his back seven struggles in coverage. Metcalf may be sacrificed here, as he was in Week 3 (31 yards) and last year (49 and 50 yards). The good news is the Steelers project for 23.0 points (14th) as slight dogs in a highly important game.

We'll see if A.J. Brown is going to play. He's at least traveling with the team. The matchup is clean against the Lions' pass funnel defense either way. Detroit has allowed the most passing YPG (277).

30. Jakobi Meyers (at TEN) - 40.5 proj. receiving yards

Meyers has operated as an efficient complementary weapon in Jacksonville's passing attack. He's the WR45 on WR48 usage this season and his targets have slightly jumped to 5.3 per game over the past month. Jacksonville spreads things out too much for Meyers to be a high-value asset, but he is a red-zone option and can beat up on bad opponents.

He now draws the league's most favorable positional matchup, as Tennessee ranks first in half PPR points allowed per game to wide receivers. The Titans are giving up huge numbers across all depths, ranking first in points allowed to intermediate throws and second to passes under 10 air yards, where Meyers has logged 27 and 13 targets, respectively, this season. The Jaguars are 10.5-point road favorites with a healthy implied team total of 28.5 points (third). There are worse WR3 dart throws during bye weeks and at the peak of injuries.

33. Jordyn Tyson (vs. CAR) - 40.5 proj. receiving yards

34. Khalil Shakir (at NYJ) - 42.5 proj. receiving yards

Shakir is Steady Eddie in this awesome Bills offense. He's scored between 7.8 and 11.4 half PPR points in 5 straight games. Over the past month, he's the WR37 on WR37 receptions per game. Shakir is the primary underneath target but on occasion can spring loose for an explosive. It's harder for him to score touchdowns in this run-heavy offense in the red zone, keeping his ceiling in check.

This is a clean spot for Shakir's role. The Jets rank 24th in half PPR points allowed to opposing WR rooms, giving up 23.2 points per game. However, New York has been generous against underneath looks, ranking seventh in points allowed to shallow targets under 10 air yards, where Shakir has seen 76% of his targets this season. Buffalo enters as 8.5-point road favorites with a 29.5-point team total (first).

35. Antonio Williams (at NYG) - 37.5 proj. receiving yards

Williams ran a route on 40 of 45 dropbacks last week but it only resulted in six targets. One of them was an explosive where he traveled through the A-gap after being wound up in motion. He can stretch the field vertically and run a slot fade for Jayden Daniels. There is more upside here than his recent box scores would suggest. In three games without Terry McLaurin, Williams is averaging 6.7 targets per game (WR30).

A date with the Giants is going to be a battle. He'll see some of CB1 Deonte Banks, who has been a monster in man coverage. They're well-coached, with experience in the secondary and DROY Arvell Reese bringing pressure from off-ball LB. Williams was in a part-time role against them back in Week 5, while McLaurin finished as the WR25 on 62 scoreless yards. Beyond his individual matchup, a 19.0-point team total by the Commanders works against his fantasy appeal, aside from WR4 darts.

36. Jordan Addison (at GB) - 42.5 proj. receiving yards

I wrote about Addison and Kirk Cousins here.

37. Caleb Douglas (at IND) - 41.5 proj. receiving yards

38. Xavier Hutchinson (at CLE) - 27.5 proj. receiving yards

Hugging Face CEO vows to keep fighting for open-source AI after Jensen Huang’s exit

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Hugging Face CEO Clément Delangue briefly commented on Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s departure on Thursday, calling it a “really sad day.” Nvidia had announced its plan to acquire the platform for open-source AI models in September for $12.9 billion, making it the second-largest acquisition in the company’s history, just behind that of chipmaker Groq’s assets for $20 billion in December 2025.

“This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day! But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!,” Delangue wrote on X.

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!

The post is interesting as it implies that Huang’s departure is potentially worrying to open-source advocates, and he’s attempting to offer reassurance.

As Nvidia’s CEO, Huang was known as a strong advocate for open-source AI and open-weight models, which is why there could be concerns about how a company that’s no longer under his leadership will now approach this space.

After Nvidia announced its plans to acquire Hugging Face, the U.S. Department of Justice began making inquiries about how the deal would impact competition in the AI market, Reuters reported in October. While Nvidia pledged that the platform would preserve open support, it would also allow the company to control a major distribution channel for AI models.

The Hugging Face deal is still scheduled to close in the first half of 2027. Requests for further comment on the deal status and whether Hugging Face has spoken yet with Nvidia’s interim CEO Collette Kress were not returned.

Huang’s advocacy for open-source and open-weight AI has long extended beyond Hugging Face. In July, the CEO published an AI policy letter as his first-ever post on the social network X. The letter, which was signed by 25 companies, including Microsoft, Meta, Hugging Face, IBM, Palantir, and others, urged the U.S. government not to over-restrict open-weight AI models. It said models that “anyone can download, inspect, modify, and run on their own infrastructure” are an important part of the AI ecosystem because they make AI “more accessible, adaptable, and widely available.”

Huang also wrote that open-weight models expand access to the AI economy, strengthen competition, and give customers greater control, as they know they won’t become locked into a single provider. “Open models strengthen safety and cybersecurity, accelerate innovation and diffusion, and enable sovereignty,” his post on X stated.

For my first post, I’m sharing a letter @NVIDIA signed on why open models matter.AI will transform every industry, power every company, and be built by every country.Open models strengthen safety and cybersecurity, accelerate innovation and diffusion, and enable sovereignty.… pic.twitter.com/t02bi51N4C

In August, Nvidia also announced a $6 billion deal with the coding startup Poolside to help build out an open AI ecosystem in the U.S. to help the country compete against Chinese companies like DeepSeek and Kimi K3, The Wall Street Journal reported at the time. Last month, Nvidia expanded its Nemotron family of open-weight models with a large 120-billion-parameter model for agents used by the enterprise as well as with two new lightweight Bolt models that run on PCs.

Earlier this year, Huang promoted open-source AI during his January keynote address at the Consumer Electronics Show in Las Vegas, noting that open-source models have “really revolutionized artificial intelligence.

Pochettino Has 'Always Loved' Marcus Rashford, He Can Be His Next Dele Alli

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino is closing in on being the next Manchester United manager after Michael Carrick was sacked on Thursday morning, and he will already be thinking about the squad he is inheriting.

Man United finally confirmed the dismissal of Carrick on Thursday, despite it being widely known that he was set to go since Monday, before Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada travelled to Monaco to make the recommendation to co-owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe that he should be relieved of his duties.

It's also been no secret that Red Devils chiefs have been working on the appointment of Pochettino this week while Carrick has continued to take training, with GIVEMESPORT sources revealing on Wednesday night that the club had reached an agreement in principle with the Argentine over personal terms.

United are now ready to pay the $10m release clause in his USMNT contract, with a full agreement now edging closer ahead of their match against Haiti on Saturday.

Pochettino is currently expected to take charge of that match, but it is so far unclear whether he will be in the dugout for the second-leg against the same nation next Wednesday.

United's next match is against Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday, and the 54-year-old could be without some key experienced players for the trip to Anfield, with fitness doubts over Luke Shaw, Harry Maguire and Marcus Rashford.

According to a new report from Telegraph journalist Matt Law, Pochettino has 'always loved' the latter and will see him as one of his key players at Old Trafford.

A former colleague of Pochettino told the reporter:

Mauricio has always loved Rashford. He will put an arm around him and give him that same kind of love he showed to Dele Alli [at Spurs]. Then it’s up to Rashford.

After a stunning loan spell with Barcelona last season, and with the player previously unsettled at Old Trafford, the most likely outcome in the summer seemed like it would be a permanent move away for Rashford.

Instead, the LaLiga giants decided to sign Anthony Gordon from Newcastle United instead, and the 29-year-old didn't receive any offers that tempted him to leave the club.

Carrick said he was pleased to have the England forward back at the club, and in a remarkable turnaround, he began the season in the starting XI for the Red Devils.

However, Rashford struggled to make a meaningful impact before suffering a hip injury that is keeping him on the sidelines.

There may be some optimism that he could be available against Liverpool, but even if he isn't, he looks set to get an arm around the shoulder from Pochettino.

Dele Alli's career has ultimately gone downhill since he left Tottenham, but when he was at the club with Pochettino, he was one of the best players in the Premier League.

There was probably a touch of tough love from the Argentine as he looked to get the very best out of Alli, and Rashford could be in for the same treatment given Pochettino is clearly a huge fan of him.

Rashford must now take this opportunity with both hands and truly relaunch his Old Trafford career.

After initial contacts, detailed transfer talks are expected as United prepare to alter their roster before next month.

Welch’s holiday microdrama embraces Y2K nostalgia as Gen Z become parents

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The five-episode series features the actress who voiced Kim in “Kim Possible” and a member of The Cheetah Girls group.

Welch’s is jumping on the microdrama trend as consumers head into the holiday season. “Before the Toast” features many of the trappings typically associated with the format — like absurdly high stakes and plenty of drama — and a nostalgia-fueled cast to promote the brand’s line of sparkling juices during a time of year when demand is usually high.

Joining Romano in the series is her husband, Brendan Rooney, Sabrina Bryan of The Cheetah Girls and Heather Morris, from “Glee.” Storylines largely revolve around navigating holiday season chaos with young children and creating new memories.

The actors chosen for the campaign, in addition to its strategic placement around family-friend holiday programming, may help the brand reach families with younger children. The demand for sparkling juices has gone up as consumers look for alternatives to soda and alcoholic beverages. This season also sees the introduction of Welch’s Sparkling Low Sugar Grape, which is available at Walmart.

The cast may also be nostalgic for Gen Z parents who grew up on Disney Channel shows, many of whom are sharing the media with their own children.

As part of the campaign, the brand teamed up with Instacart to give away full-size bottles of its products in the lead-up to Thanksgiving and Christmas. Shoppers are able to claim the offer via the product card on the Instacart app.

Microdramas are being embraced by brands looking to reach consumers. The format has proven popular with Gen Z, who have grown used to watching short, vertical videos on their phones. The scale of efforts have varied, from just a few episodes to P&G’s 50-part series for Native. Welch’s isn’t the first beverage brand to jump on the microdrama bandwagon. In May, Dr Pepper promoted the return of its Creamy Coconut product with a four-episode series in which a young woman has a fleeting love affair with a can of the dirty soda-inspired flavor.

Welch’s, which operates as an agricultural cooperative and does not release earnings statements, replaced its CMO position with a chief brand and innovation officer in October of last year. Andrew Hartshorn, who previously held roles at PepsiCo and Danone, assumed the role. Shortly after he arrived, the brand introduced a new kid mascot, marking the first time in 25 years that Welch’s creative starred a child character.

Sadness is the general sentiment, it seems.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Welp, that's it, the face of Nvidia is no longer the face of Nvidia. Jensen Huang served as CEO of the company since it was founded over three decades ago, and now he's out, replaced by the new interim CEO Colette Kress, who was previously Chief Financial Officer (exactly what we predicted, by the way).

This is in the wake of two affair scandals surrounding the former CEO. The first affair Huang admitted to, and it's alleged that the other party in the tango is a lady who is currently accused of being a Chinese spy. The second is a reported affair that Huang allegedly had with Nvidia's VP of healthcare, Kimberly Powell.

Many people expected Huang's resignation in the lead-up to this, so it is no big surprise that it has now been announced by Nvidia. But now that it's official, the response has largely been as expected: a combination of disappointment at his resignation and praise for his time as Nvidia CEO.

President Trump certainly expressed as much, saying Huang has done a "fantastic job." Trump also has a suggestion for who should take over: ex-Apple CEO Tim Cook: "Now that Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, who has done a fantastic job, has resigned, there is only one man to take his place - Tim Cook of Apple. A Legend replaces a Legend! President Donald J. Trump."

Hugging Face co-founder Clement Delangue expresses the general sentiment: "This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!"

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day! But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!November 12, 2026

Nvidia recently set out to buy Hugging Face, the open-source AI platform, and given the latter agreed, it makes sense for the company to assuage any fears over the news of Huang's resignation. Julien Chaumond, Hugging Face co-founder and CTO, has expressed that "the mission has not and will not change."

Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney has expressed his sadness, too: "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity. Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction."

Sweeney's response isn't a surprise. Before Huang's resignation was announced, both Tim Sweeney and Elon Musk certainly expressed high praise for Huang. The former said, "This would be an Apple firing Steve Jobs level of idiocy from Nvidia’s board if they did that, and a grievous harm to the United States of America." And the latter said it would be "an honor" to hire him.

It's also no surprise, then, that the Epic CEO has banged the same drum a few additional times since the announcement, calling it "far too much government heavy handedness" and taking a dig at Nvidia's new interim CEO for waiting a week until discussing everything in a company meeting.

Even ancient file-sharing platform LimeWire has jumped in on the news.

Over the past couple of days before the resignation, there were differing opinions on whether Huang should resign or not. Some thought so, but the more general sentiment across social media seemed to be that regardless of any alleged affairs, he is a good CEO and it will be very damaging for Nvidia to lose him.

The reactions now, in the wake of Huang's resignation, seem to concur.

That is, apart from the US Select Committee on China, which is decidedly more stern:

"Jensen Huang’s firing from the company he founded is an unfortunate end that could have been avoided. It is in our national interest for NVIDIA and our other AI champions to succeed, but American companies should never jeopardize our nation’s security by giving Chinese companies access to their most advanced chips. Nvidia must now change its corporate practices and appoint leadership that makes national security its top priority. In Congress we must act quickly to pass bipartisan export control legislation in the year-end national defense bill."

As far as we're concerned as PC gamers, much of this might be by-the-by. Whoever heads up Nvidia, the company long since stopped putting gaming anywhere near the centre of its focus. AI is the name of the game, now.

I can't help but feel a little sad, though. Huang has been one of the biggest names in PC gaming hardware for decades, but no longer. Whatever you think of the man himself and whether he should have resigned, there is indeed something sad about his era being over.

How To Make Money With Water Trading In Probably Stolen

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

How to access, test, clean, and sell water to turn a profit in Probably Stolen.

Water trading in Probably Stolen is exactly what it sounds like; you're buying and selling water. As simple as that sounds, there's actually quite a bit of thought that goes into it. You need a consistent source for your water, a way to ensure and improve its quality, ways to store it, and information on when it's going to be most valuable.

Here's everything you need to know to successfully become a water trader in Probably Stolen.

When you first start the game, the only way to get water is to buy it from people who come to your window. While this works for the first few days, if you're wanting to scale your water trading, you'll need some way to have constant access to water.

You can get this by purchasing the Water Trading upgrade from Joe Wild's network for 300 credits, then the Gutterflow On Tap upgrade for 250 credits. This gives you a tap directly in your store with constant access to Gutterflow with variable purity depending on the day and a small amount of water trading supplies including the water trading manual, a pitcher with a filter, testing strips, purification tablets, and a few bottles.

You can access these perks automatically if you choose to start your run with the Water Merchant scenario.

As mentioned, water has varying grades that affect who will buy it and what it can be used for.

Most customers will only buy water that has a Basewater quality or higher. You can sometimes fudge the label with the labelmaker, but keep in mind that most scavengers and people from the higher levels will have ways to test the water and catch you in a lie, which can affect your reputation. Because of this, it's best to try and get the water to genuinely match the higher purity levels.

How to start growing your own goods and turning a profit in Probably Stolen.

You can do this with various cleaning tools. Start by testing the water with a test strip or Aquascan; test strips are one-time use while the Aquascan is reusable, but they both tell you what purity level your water is currently sitting at. Once tested, you can use a variety of tools to filter and clean the water.

Beyond these tools, you might also consider picking up some storage options. A Bottle Printer can make sure you have storage containers available at all times while a Water Tank gives you 20,000 mL of storage or 50,000 mL with expansion. You can get both from Dr. Jackson or the Retired Water Merchant.

Water sales are pretty profitable all the time in Probably Stolen, but can be even more valuable in the event of a water shortage, usually caused by the water treatment plant on the station going down. You can sometimes know in advance if this is going to happen by paying the Shady Customer that comes to your window every week or so 50 credits.

If you have enough notice, you can produce a large amount of inventory and put water in your showcase to attract more customers looking for it. Try to keep a storage bay filled with water during these events. You can track how long you'll need supplies in your calendar at the beginning of every day.

Which upgrades should you invest in at the beginning of your next Probably Stolen run for more money or better perks later on?

Trump opens door to immigration deal, but Republicans have one condition

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"Obama did this very thing. They still call him the 'deporter in chief'. And he did oversee a lot of deportations. But those numbers were also cooked. The books were cooked because they were counting things as deportations that were, in fact, just turnaways at the border and many other things baked into those Obama numbers. But then what he did when he tried to push his Dreamers and all his things, and he said, look, I've deported all these people. I've done what you wanted with that. Now we need to start looking at amnesty and legal status. But this is the exact same thing that we're seeing today.

Some Senate Republicans are open to revisiting immigration policy after President Donald Trump floated a possible deal with Democrats, but they insist amnesty remains off the table.

Republicans, after sweeping midterm losses, are willing to open up the immigration policy debate after voters made clear that they didn't support the administration’s approach: a Fox News poll published in September found that 53% of voters believed that Trump's immigration policy had gone too far.

But there’s one thing that Senate Republicans won’t agree to: amnesty.

WHITE HOUSE DOUBLES DOWN AFTER TRUMP ANGERS BASE BY FLOATING ILLEGAL ALIEN DEAL WITH DEMS

President Donald Trump arrives to deliver remarks during a campaign rally at the Rock Hill Sports and Event Center in Rock Hill, S.C., on Oct. 29, 2026. Trump was in South Carolina rallying support for local Republican candidates ahead of the midterm elections. (Getty Images)

"I trust President Trump," Sen. Jim Banks, R-Ind., told Fox News Digital. "He's gonna do what's right, but as far as I'm concerned, amnesty is off the table. I'll never go for it."

The door creaked open on the idea when Trump, during an interview with "60 Minutes" that aired over the weekend, said that his administration’s mass deportation and enforcement policies might be "too tough."

"Get the killers out, get the rapists out, get all the rough ones out, get the drug dealers out," Trump said. "But let the people that are here — and in some cases here for 10 and 15 years — work a deal for them."

The administration quickly sought to clarify Trump’s comments, with Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin affirming that there would be "no amnesty" shortly after.

HOUSE GOP ERUPTS AT STEPHEN MILLER AS TRUMP ELEVATES HIM AFTER MIDTERM MASSACRE: 'MEGALOMANIAC'

Still, Republicans are willing to navigate the complex issue as Democrats gear up to take power next year. Some have been calling for action on the matter for months.

Sen. Thom Tillis, R-N.C., one of the administration’s sharper Republican critics on immigration policy who is retiring at the end of the year, told Fox News Digital that as early as March he texted Trump and suggested it would be the "perfect time" to codify "all the stuff that has effectively sealed the border and used it as an opportunity to get merit-based immigration reform."

"I also said in that text, though, was, in order for this to be successful, Stephen Miller either needs to be out of the room or on his meds," Tillis said. "And so he's the reason they're walking it back is because they don't want to do it, because they have some false assumption that that's gonna hurt our base."

"Our base is fine with merit-based immigration reform. President Trump is fine with merit-based immigration reform," he continued. "And if we don't get the stuff codified that has effectively sealed the border, it's gonna be on them when the border gets opened up if a Democrat wins in ‘28."

The Senate, just before Trump won the 2024 election, was on the cusp of a bipartisan immigration deal when Democrats controlled the chamber. The deal collapsed after Trump criticized it and Senate Republicans withdrew support.

"There's a lot of scar tissue on this issue, but, you know, the president, because he secured the borders, he has credibility on the issue, and he has expressed a willingness," Senate Majority Leader John Thune, R-S.D., said. "I have not seen anything yet that's being proposed, but we will certainly hear them out, and if there's something he wants to do there, have a conversation about it."

JEFFRIES VOWS DEMOCRATS WON’T ‘GIVE A DIME’ TO TRUMP’S DEPORTATION EFFORTS

Sen. James Lankford, R-Okla., accused Senate Democrats of being more afraid of "ICE than they are of Iran." (Allison Robbert/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

The bipartisan deal was the product of negotiations among Sens. James Lankford, R-Okla., Chris Murphy, D-Conn., and former Sen. Kyrsten Sinema, and would have funneled billions for border security and shuttered the border to most asylum seekers, among several other policy changes. But conservatives balked at the deal, and Trump’s influence from the campaign trail ultimately killed it.

Years later, Lankford is again in a prime position to work on a deal with Democrats, given that he is expected to become the ranking Republican on the Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee next year.

"If I’m ranking on Homeland Security, I’m right in the thick of it, whether you like it or not," Lankford said.

Though opinions vary on what exactly the GOP’s approach would be going forward, many Republicans agree that the administration’s enforcement policies, particularly under former Homeland Security Secretary Kristi Noem, should not make a comeback.

"I think you have to make sure, first and foremost, that how you do your enforcement is not like the administration started under Kristi Noem," Sen. Ted Budd, R-N.C., told Fox News Digital.

"I think people are for some enforcement, but not in a cruel way. And there were so many, there was a lack of training, lack of proper methodologies, and I think people want to take enforcement seriously."

"But, again, I would say no amnesty at all," he continued. "We don't want to go there. Ronald Reagan, a great president, one of his few regrets, but significant regrets, was amnesty. And I don't think Trump wants to make that mistake."

Alex Miller is a writer for Fox News Digital covering the U.S. Senate.

England vs Croatia: Prediction, kick-off time, TV, live stream, team news, h2h results, odds today

England face Croatia tonight in the penultimate game of the Nations League group stage, with captain Harry Kane to become their most-capped men’s player of all time.

Fresh from winning the Ballon d’Or in London last month, Kane is set to make his 126th senior international appearance on Thursday evening to move clear of long-time record-holder Peter Shilton.

CLICK HERE TO FOLLOW ENGLAND VS CROATIA LIVE!

Croatia head to Wembley with their mauling at the hands of the Three Lions in Rijeka last month still fresh in the memory.

Thomas Tuchel’s side put seven past their hosts without reply, as Kane and Bukayo Saka both bagged braces. There were further goals from Jude Bellingham, Anthony Gordon and Morgan Rogers in a match played technically behind closed doors, but with thousands of schoolchildren permitted to attend.

England are poised to advance to the next stage of the Nations League but currently via a second-placed finish, which would set up a quarter-final with a big hitter such as France or Portugal.

Victory for England at Wembley would likely tee up a mouthwatering last-game decider away in Spain on Sunday, with the winner of that match topping the group.

England vs Croatia is scheduled for a 7.45pm GMT kick-off tonight on Thursday November 12, 2026.

The match will take place at Wembley Stadium in north-west London.

TV channel: In the UK, England vs Croatia in the 2026/27 Nations League is being televised live and free to air on ITV, with coverage of the match beginning at 7pm on ITV1.

Live stream: Catch the game live online through the ITVX website and app, which is free with a registration.

Live blog: You can follow all the action with Standard Sport’s live blog, featuring expert insight and analysis from England correspondents Dom Smith and Sam Tabuteau.

Injuries to Marc Guehi, Djed Spence and Ezri Konsa have led to late England call-ups for Dan Burn and James Hill, with the latter hoping to make his senior debut.

Other potential debutants include Fulham's Josh King and Borussia Dortmund midfielder Jobe Bellingham, younger brother of England star Jude Bellingham.

England are a bit light on the right flank with Arsenal duo Saka and Noni Madueke both not in the squad, with Chelsea’s Rogers or Liverpool youngster Rio Ngumoha likely to start instead.

For Croatia, ex-Tottenham winger Ivan Perisic is suspended after his red card last time out against Spain in Split.

After last month’s chastening 7-0 loss to England, many in Croatia are writing this game off, with calls to rest legendary 41-year-old Luka Modric for the crunch clash with the Czech Republic on Sunday.

As mentioned above, this fixture has already produced plenty of goals. Croatia have been leaky at the back throughout the Nations League campaign, conceding 14 times in four games.

A 4-1 drubbing at the hands of Spain in their last away match indicates that England should have plenty of chances to score at home.

While the Three Lions were able to keep Croatia quiet in Rijeka, their guests on Thursday night usually score at least once. It would not be a surprise to see England match Spain’s latest result against Croatia.

England and Croatia have crossed paths in three of the last four major tournaments, with the Three Lions victorious on two occasions. However, Croatia had the last laugh - beating England in the semi-finals of the World Cup in Russia in 2018.

Overall, this is a fixture England usually win - but Croatia have inflicted major upsets before, preventing England from qualifying for Euro 2008 with wins home and away.

Four excellent Garmin watches are on deep discount ahead of Black Friday

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Garmin has a watch for every budget this year, and it’s clear the company is working on shipping more watches than ever at these prices.

After hunting for smartwatch deals over the past few weeks, it’s pretty clear Garmin isn’t playing around this year. I’ve found several older Garmin watches on sale this month, but now the company is extending the early Black Friday sales to some of its newest watches. Let’s start with those first.

The Garmin Forerunner 70 is the first budget-minded Forerunner watch in five years, and the white colorway is currently $50 off (20%), coming in at just $199, its lowest price ever. The Forerunner 70 is a fantastic-looking watch that packs the same Garmin OS as the more expensive models, including new Quick Workout features, 50 more workout modes, and an upgraded optical heart rate sensor and GPS modules over the previous budget Forerunner watch, the Forerunner 55.

The second new Garmin watch on sale today is the Venu 4 bundle, which is currently $105 off (19%), making it $454.99, this watch’s lowest price ever. The Silver/Gray colorway is the least expensive one. The slate model with a brown leather band is also $105 off, and while that model is normally the most expensive one anyway, this sale makes it $504.99. No matter which colorway you choose, the bundle includes:

The Venu 4 is the latest in Garmin’s most elegant line of smartwatches, packing a 1.4-inch circular AMOLED screen inside a stainless steel case. It tracks heart rate, Pulse Ox, HRV status, stress, women’s health (with skin temperature readings), advanced sleep with circadian alignment, and puts it all together in a Body Battery energy score so you can know what days to push harder (and which ones you really need to take it easy on).

If you’re OK with something just a little bit older, the Venu 3S and Fenix E are impressively solid choices at terrific prices:

I covered these deals a few days ago, and they’re somehow still around, which is impressive considering they’ve never been cheaper. The Fenix E features multi-GNSS navigation plus a 3-axis compass, gyroscope, barometric altimeter, pulse ox monitoring, respiration tracking, advanced sleep monitoring, real-time stamina tracking, and a training readiness score to tie it all together. All of this happens despite the watch lasting two weeks per charge.

And the Venu 3S is still loved by a ton of people in the Garmin community thanks to its gorgeous trim, lighter weight than the Venu 4, better battery life (by 1-2 days), and an OS some people prefer over Garmin’s newer designs. These watches are built to last you a long time, so feel good about investing in one, especially if you’re serious about staying healthy.

Save $119 on this 3-item Newegg combo that includes 32GB of Corsair Vengeance DDR5-6000 CL36 RAM, the awesome-for-gaming Ryzen 5 9800X3D CPU, and a premium motherboard with MSI's X870E Carbon Wifi.

The X3D variant's 96MB of L3 cache helps with gaming (among other things), making it one of the fastest gaming CPUs available. The all-black motherboard sports a cool RGB design element of the MSI dragon on the VRM heatsink to break up the monotony and lend some bling to the inside of your chassis. It’s a great motherboard to build your AM5 rig around.

In the end, we like this combo as this, and others like it, help take some of the pain out of buying a PC today. England XI vs Croatia: Predicted lineup, confirmed team news, injury latest for Nations League today

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

England take on Croatia again in the Nations League tonight, on what will be a memorable evening for Harry Kane at Wembley.

The Three Lions captain will move clear of Peter Shilton as his country’s most-capped men’s player of all time on his 126th senior appearance, while also showcasing his newly-won Ballon d’Or award.

CLICK HERE TO FOLLOW ENGLAND VS CROATIA LIVE!

Kane is expected to lead Thomas Tuchel’s attack as usual against a team that were thrashed 7-0 by England in Rijeka last month, supported by Anthony Gordon, Jude Bellingham and potentially Rio Ngumoha.

Manchester City centre-back Marc Guehi was assessed over the weekend after being substituted with a hip injury at half-time of Saturday’s defeat by Nottingham Forest and has since pulled out of the England squad.

Tuchel called up Dan Burn in his place, having also now handed a first senior call-up to Bournemouth’s James Hill.

Arsenal defender Ezri Konsa will not be available for the Croatia game, but is expected to return to full training ahead of Sunday’s trip to Spain.

He has been following his own individualised programme while the rest of the squad train at Tottenham’s Hotspur Way.

Tuchel ruled Konsa out of Croatia’s visit, along with Inter Milan full-back Djed Spence, who is also injured but should be in contention to face Spain.

Tuchel must decide whether to hand Hill a senior debut or reshuffle his defence with Ben White coming in at right-back, thus allowing Jarell Quansah to partner Burn in the middle.

The injured Bukayo Saka and Arsenal team-mate Noni Madueke are not in the squad this month, with Tuchel explaining that he sees his right-wing options as being Ngumoha, Morgan Rogers, Morgan Gibbs-White and Josh King.

Declan Rice is back in the mix and available after missing the last international break due to load management.

Jobe Bellingham, younger brother of Jude, is in the senior England squad for the first time. He, King and veteran Brighton goalkeeper Jason Steele are the only uncapped players called up by Tuchel.

"We believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

We're now one week away from the launch of GTA 6, and Palworld studio Pocketpair has announced that it'll be giving devs excited to play Rockstar's game the time off to do so, because "we believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention."

I don't know about you, but I suspect that November 19 may see a lot of people around the world calling in sick to their jobs thanks to a sudden bout of illness that's going to take them until at least after the weekend to recover from. Pocketpair might be anticipating that itself, as a staff notice also shared on Twitter confirms that they'll all be able to "undertake a business trip to Vice City," if they please.

"On November 19, millions of gamers around the world will find themselves sitting at school or work, gazing longingly out the window, knowing that a new era of gaming is about to begin," the notice starts. "At Pocketpair, we believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention.

"Therefore," it continues, "after careful consideration, we have decided that all employees who intend to play Grand Theft Auto VI will be granted a special five-day holiday." That period, of course, runs from November 19 to November 23, during which "employees will be excused from their regular duties and are instead encouraged to undertake a business trip to Vice City. We hope everyone enjoys their time away from the office and returns safely from their travels."

All Pocketpair employees have been granted special leave to attend to an urgent five-day business trip in Vice City when GTA6 launches. Please see the attached notice for details. https://t.co/qJjJ7IvhhYNovember 12, 2026

I genuinely thought that this was a joke post at first, but separate tweets from Pocketpair CEO Takuro Mizobe and head of publishing John "Bucky" Buckley indicate otherwise. Mizobe says (via machine translation) that he'll be taking time off to play GTA 6 himself, while Bucky admits that "things might be a bit quiet" while the team is busy playing Rockstar's new title.

Bucky previously joked that Slay the Spire 2's release had delayed Palworld's 1.0 launch by "at least a whole day" after Mizobe got a telling off for staying up all night to play it, and I'd imagine the company might want to avoid a similar situation here. Better than having everyone in the office getting no work done, right?

Pocketpair wants to make it clear that the GTA 6 holiday won't affect any of its schedules, though: "Please note that update work for Palworld and Craftopia is proceeding smoothly and is expected to be carried out as scheduled," it adds. Let the devs have their fun, basically.

Nvidia's share price has seemingly grown by around 10% since pre-market trading lows.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

This week has been a mess for Nvidia, with its CEO, Jensen Huang, admitting to an affair on Sunday, only for a second affair allegation to come out yesterday. Today, Nvidia shares started as low as $151 in pre-market trading, but have since bounced back to between $163 and $166 in the opening hours of Nasdaq trading after the announcement of Jensen Huang's retirement as CEO.

One might assume a downward trajectory with Huang's resignation, and CFO Colette Kress replacing him, as it could flag instability to shareholders, but the stock price now sits where it once sat on Wednesday, November 11 at 5 PM ET. The Nvidia stock price has been unruly this week, dipping by $15 between Friday night and Monday morning, then climbing to $181 by the end of Monday, then once again dropping to $167 on Tuesday.

But, importantly, this morning saw the worst trade open for Nvidia stock since June 2025, just before the memory crisis started. The jump from $151 to around $165 represents a 10% increase for the company.

Naturally, stocks are a fickle business, and, famously, the 'dead cat bounce' effect describes a moment in a stock decline where it briefly recovers. But Nvidia, even with a big loss in market cap value, is still an incredibly successful company worth multiple trillions of dollars at the time of writing.

I had my eye on the trading value this morning, somewhat expecting a decline after the opening, given the drop overnight, and it's easy to see why. Jensen Huang is a cofounder of Nvidia, which at one point was the most valuable company in the world. He has been the president and CEO of the company since 1993, and he's no doubt been instrumental in the business and its success.

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!November 12, 2026

And this morning, many big names in the tech industry expressed their sadness at his departure. Tim Sweeney, the Epic Games founder and CEO, said it was "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity", and Clement Delangue, the co-founder and CEO of AI platform Hugging Face, said, "This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day". Jim Cramer, the host of Mad Money on CNBC, said "Jensen we hardly knew yah. Just plain awful...."

Colette Kress has taken over, but only in the interim, and Nvidia is no doubt shopping around for the person who will replace Huang. Bets are open on who exactly that person could be, from the CEO of Intel, AMD, and Arm, all the way to Elon Musk. And Donald Trump even wants ex-Apple CEO Tim Cook to take the chair. It's anyone's guess, really, and a good sign that it's also hard to guess exactly what will happen when it comes to stock prices.

Bari, sciolto il Comitato dei Garanti: "Impossibile portare avanti il nostro lavoro"

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Protagonista negli ultimi giorni di un botta e risposta con la proprietà del Bari, il Comitato dei Garanti è stato ufficialmente sciolto nella giornata di oggi a distanza di cinque mesi dalla sua nascita che, nei piani del sindaco Vito Leccese, doveva accompagnare il percorso per il superamento della multiproprietà, la cessione del club e garantire alla città il massimo livello di informazione, trasparenza e vigilanza sul rispetto degli impegni assunti dalla proprietà del club.

Come riporta Telebari infatti lo scioglimento del Comitato era nell’aria da qualche giorno, proprio a seguito delle parole del portavoce Sassanelli che aveva lamentato la mancata e incompleta trasmissione della documentazione chiesta alla proprietà del Bari. Oggi i membri del comitato si sono incontrati con il sindaco e al termine dell’incontro è stato annunciato lo scioglimento del gruppo.

A parlare dopo l’incontro è stato l’ex presidente della Corte d’Appello di Bari Francesco Cassano, uno dei membri del gruppo: “L'esito di quest'incontro era inevitabile. Credo che il comitato sia stato messo nell'impossibilità di operare utilmente. Utilmente significava, alla luce degli accordi tra la società e l'amministrazione comunale, capire se si intendesse effettivamente avviare un percorso che portasse alla vendita della società massimo entro il 2027. Non siamo in grado di poter accertare questo scopo. Conseguentemente non c'è una ragione per cui dobbiamo proseguire nel nostro impegno. - ha proseguito Cassano come riporta Tuttobari.com - Siamo grati al sindaco perché ci ha chiamati. Abbiamo provato a dare una mano per risolvere un problema molto delicato. Abbiamo fatto tutto il possibile per tentare di avviare un dialogo vero con la società. Riteniamo che i punti di vista siano legittimamente diversi. Abbiamo inoltre riscontrato la volontà di non collaborare pienamente secondo quelle che erano le nostre necessità. Abbiamo tentato già a luglio di avviare un dialogo con la società e non abbiamo avuto risposta. Poi finite le vicissitudini giudiziarie che hanno coinvolto la società, pensavamo potesse partire un dialogo proficuo.

Il 17 ottobre c'è stato un importante incontro, in cui abbiamo richiesto la documentazione iniziale ai rappresentanti della società. Ebbene anche quella non ci è stata fornita per intero e poi sono emerse delle inadempienze di fondo. La società ritiene che noi non abbiamo dei poteri e che sia essa stessa a decidere ciò che ci è sufficiente per svolgere il nostro compito".

Stephen Curry suffers oblique abdominal tear, will be re-evaluated in two weeks.

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The NBA injury news continues to pile up as the Golden State Warriors announced that Stephen Curry has suffered an oblique abdominal tear and will miss at least two weeks.

The two-time MVP and 12-time All-Star suffered the injury early in the first quarter of Wednesday night's game against the Houston Rockets with a little under five minutes elapsed in the game. Curry came off a screen and hit a three-pointer but grimaced in pain as he released the shot. He immediately signaled to the bench and, after the Warriors were able to call a timeout, Curry went into the locker room in visible pain. By the second quarter, the 38-year-old guard was already ruled out for the remainder of the game with an abdominal injury and was sent for an MRI during the second half of the game, which the Warriors lost 115-108.

After the game. Warriors head coach Steve Kerr said "there was a high level of concern" about Curry's status and relayed that Curry told him that this pain was something the 38-year-old had never felt before.

Given the level of concern the Warriors seemed to have at the end of Wednesday's game, the diagnosis could be taken as relatively good news. Curry will miss at least the next seven games, but there is a chance that's all. While the Magic's Paolo Banchero missed 34 games with an oblique injury last season, the most recent examples of NBA oblique injuries put the return-to-play timeline around 2-3 weeks. That means Curry could be back by the start of December.

While no injury to a player like Curry comes at a good time, this is a particularly tough moment for the Warriors to be forced to be without their star. Despite playing without Jimmy Butler (knee surgery), Moses Moody (knee surgery), and Kristaps Porzingis (illness), the Warriors had seemingly found their groove. They came into Wednesday night having won eight in a row. Curry was averaging 27.8 points, 3.7 rebounds, and 3.9 assists per game while shooting 50% from the field and 45.7% from beyond the arc. His elite play, along with the emergence of Yaxel Lendeborg, had made the Warriors a contender in the Western Conference, even without Butler.

It was a much-needed hot stretch after the Warriors missed the playoffs last year following a 37-45 season. Curry missed 39 games with injuries last season as well, and the Warriors were just 13-26 without him, which means they will likely have some work to do to make up ground once Curry is able to return.

In the meantime, even more will fall on the plate of Lendeborg, but Kerr will be cautious not to overload the rookie. Brandin Podziemski will be able to handle more of the scoring load, and it appears that De'Anthony Melton is the best bet to enter the starting lineup for Curry. In 25 minutes last night against the Rockets, Melton had 11 points, four rebounds, and four assists.

Given the significant injuries suffered by the Thunder, Trail Blazers, and Jazz, the Warriors, who are currently sixth in the Western Conference, remain very much in the mix. The team will just need to keep things afloat for the next two-plus weeks until their captain can return.

This article has been updated with the most recent news.

Xavi thinks Frenkie de Jong is "one of the best midfielders in the world" and will hand him the captain's armband for the Netherlands' Nations League clash with Greece.

You Loong's Deluge Location Guide For Phantom Blade Zero

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

It's useful for when things get a little too hot in the Wulin.

Most traversal Phantom Edges in Phantom Blade Zero appear as you play through the main story, but You Loong's Deluge is different. It's not far off the beaten path, and you need it to complete one of the side quests, but it's still easy to miss if you don't explore every corner.

It's one of the more useful Phantom Edges in the game because it lets you reach areas you can't access without it. You can also use it effectively in combat, making it a great asset in Soul's arsenal. Here's how to get your hands on You Loong's Deluge.

You Loong's Deluge can be found in Pangzhen, northwest of the Green Serpent House and southwest of the Lion Dance Hall, with the latter being the closest bell. Unfortunately, it can be found in the possession of You Loong, Leader of the Blackwater Crew.

If you're coming from the Green Serpent House, you can easily walk right past You Loong, because he actually isn't visible from the main path. You have to hang a bit to the left of the main path and explore a bit before you find the wide-open space that's always indicative of a fight.

You can identify this wide-open space by a large body of water filled with candle-lit lanterns floating on the surface. As you approach, You Loong will burst from the water and initiate the fight that earns you You Loong's Deluge.

No build is complete without great accessories. Here are the 10 best in Phantom Blade Zero.

Surprisingly, You Loong isn't an exceptionally challenging fight, though that depends on your build at the time. He's not overly massive, not exceptionally fast and doesn't hit hard enough to drain your health in one strike. Most of his attacks can be parried or blocked easily, and he doesn't have many red-glint attacks you have to dodge.

One main thing to keep in mind is to take advantage of him when he dives below the water. While he's under, you can not damage each other, so it's a great opportunity to heal with medicines that replenish HP over time. After a while, he'll burst from the water and use small ranged attacks, but you can easily parry them.

If you want to attack him before he comes out of the water on his own, you can walk directly to the section of bubbles that indicates where he is, and he'll burst out regardless of where he is, using the same ranged attack as he would otherwise. It's a good way to corner him or get the jump on your next flurry of attacks.

Keep following this pattern until you've whittled down his health, staying patient and using the periods when he stays underwater. After enough cycles, you'll claim You Loong's Deluge.

You Loong's Deluge is one of the Phantom Edges in the game that's really the most beneficial depending on what build you're using. It creates a constant stream of water you can use to push enemies away while also applying the wet condition.

This means that after you use it on an enemy, they become more susceptible to both lightning and ice damage, so if you're using weapons that apply those conditions, pairing them with You Loong's Deluge will be exceptionally useful.

It's also useful against enemies that use fire weapons. After using the Deluge on one of these enemies, you will do higher damage to them, regardless of what weapons you're using.

You can also use it as a traversal item because it lets you extinguish the large, burning branches that block some pathways throughout the world, regardless of whether you have it equipped. This opens up more doors to collect some of Quick Hand Zhang's treasures and extra resources.

If you've got decision paralysis about which Phantom Edges to use, look no further.

Eminem’s Debut Release ‘Infinite’ Now Streaming for 30th Anniversary

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Eminem‘s independently released debut album, Infinite, bombed when it came out 30 years ago today. “It sold maybe 70 copies and didn’t get great feedback,” he wrote in his 2008 memoir, The Way I Am. “A lot of people said it sounded like Nas, and he was a heavy influence at the time. But I had an album out, and I could say that.” Since then, the album has taken on a legend of its own, since it caught the ears of Dr. Dre and led to Marshall Mathers’ breakthrough Slim Shady LP. The album has been reissued and bootlegged for years.

Now Infinite is getting another chance as an EP. Jake Bass and Mike “Slim” Wilder have remixed and added additional production to six of the album’s original 11 tracks for what’s now called Infinite EP. Jeff and Mark Bass, who helmed the original release, executive produced the EP, which Denaun Porter produced with Wilder. The EP is now available to stream and physical editions, which will come out on vinyl, CD, and tape on Feb. 26, can be pre-ordered. It’s unclear at the time of publication why only half of the album is being re-released.

Org Music has also produced a 10-minute documentary about the album. Jeff Bass recalls in the film how his brother Mark heard Eminem do an open mic on the radio and called the station to get the rapper to the studio. The brothers recall how Eminem showed up with D12 and started recording. There’s also archival footage of Eminem and stories from others who helped make the record.

In 1999, Eminem told Rolling Stone that the music on Infinite lacked some of the anger and humor that would make him famous on his major label debut for a specific reason. “It was right before my daughter was born, so having a future for her was all I talked about,” he said. “It was way hip-hopped out, like Nas and AZ — that rhyme style that was real in at the time. I’ve always been a smartass comedian, and that’s why it wasn’t a good album. … After that record, every rhyme I wrote got angrier and angrier.” Trending Stories Sade Are Not Attending Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Bob Dylan’s Meeting With Pope Leo: People Fixated on the Hood, But the Hands Spoke Volumes Megan Thee Stallion and Daniel Radcliffe Reunite for Season Two of ‘Reggie Dinkins’

In 2016, the Bass Brothers, Wilder, and others recalled the making of Infinite in a Rolling Stone interview. “He would come in and do a song, and he’d cut the vocal for it,” Wilder said. “He’d live with it overnight, and then he’d call me and say, ‘I’ve got to come in and cut the vocals over.’ He heard something that was wrong. So he would go back in and re-cut the vocal, and no one could hear any difference. He’d hear something that no one else could hear. That, and how raw it was, is what sticks out more than anything.”

Although Eminem has never officially re-released the full album, he did use it as the springboard for an April Fool’s joke in 2024. “The highly-anticipated next studio album goes back to where it all started,” a narrator said in a social media clip teasing Infinite 2. “Infinite but now it’s even more infinite. Out today!”

Hang On, Firefight Is Coming To Halo: Campaign Evolved

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Halo: Campaign Evolved isn't dead, people!

Firefight has just been announced as a free update for Halo: Campaign Evolved, arriving sometime this winter. The series' staple co-op PvE mode is locked in, with more details set to be shared at Halo Fest on December 12-13.

Halo: Campaign Evolved received some backlash for its lack of multiplayer options, particularly PvP and Firefight. I thought the criticism was understandable, but perhaps a little unfair given the game was always marketed as a campaign remake.

"For nearly two decades, Firefight has been a Halo favorite, challenging Spartans to team up, hold the line, and survive wave after wave of enemies. The tradition continues this winter as we add the four-player cooperative PvE mode to Halo: Campaign Evolved in a free update.

We’re kicking things off tonight at XBOX FanFest, where attendees will be the first to jump in and experience Firefight across new maps and armed with plenty of weapons to help even the odds."

Forgive my opening sentiment, but I honestly wasn't expecting anything new to come from Halo Studios. Especially after the developer was essentially reduced to a skeleton team, with future Halo production being handed over to Activision.

I'm a huge fan of Halo: Campaign Evolved, and whilst I'm still sad the teased Halo 2 remake seems unlikely, you should have seen me leap out of my seat when I spotted the announcement that Firefight was on its way. Hoorah!
